At Geom Ujin’s words, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon laughed. He had thought he would never hear such a joke from the Cult Leader in his life.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked Gyo Cheon,

“Were you not trying to draw even the Cult Leader into your plan? Well then, please show us what you have prepared.”

Gyo Cheon sneered mockingly.

“Just a moment ago, you were hiding behind that paltry blade like a coward, unable to even show your head, and now you are so bold.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon accepted the provocation with a smile.

“Because the one person Demon Venerables can rely on has arrived.”

A faint smile appeared at the corners of Geom Ujin’s mouth as well.

With the appearance of the Cult Leader, the atmosphere had eased, but inwardly, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon did not let down his guard. His master certainly had a hidden move.

But he did not send a sound transmission warning the Cult Leader to be careful.

The Cult Leader was not a perfect person, so he could make mistakes and faulty judgments. That was why there was a Chief Strategist, and why the Demon Venerables assisted him.

But there were exceptional moments when the Cult Leader became perfect. At such times, he had no need to listen to anyone’s words. Because the Cult Leader knew best.

That was precisely the moment when lives were taken and lives were lost.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon believed that, in the midst of a battlefield, the Cult Leader became perfect.

And the place where they stood now was a battlefield.

Gyo Cheon could not discern Geom Ujin’s skill. Normally, he should have felt an overwhelming presence, as he had from the previous Cult Leader. Yet Geom Ujin’s aura was difficult to read. That meant only one thing.

‘He is stronger than I expected.’

He had conducted extensive investigations on the Demonic Cult Leader and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. In particular, he had spent much time and money trying to find out the level of their martial arts.

But seeing them in person, they exceeded his investigation. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was stronger than he had thought, and so was Geom Ujin.

‘Why have they all grown so strong?’

He knew that the Young Cult Leader had brought a new wind into the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, but he could not have imagined that it had made them stronger.

At last, Geom Ujin spoke his first words to Gyo Cheon.

“Will you step forward yourself?”

He did not ask how he had been. He did not exchange any other words.

Gyo Cheon’s expression hardened at once. His first words to him were merely asking whether he would fight himself?

“You look down on me.”

Gyo Cheon showed his displeasure, but Geom Ujin said nothing more.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon could understand the Cult Leader’s heart.

In the past, Gyo Cheon had helped the First Young Master and tried to kill Geom Ujin. And now he had brought about a massacre, dragging the reputation of their cult into the dirt.

The Cult Leader must have been greatly angered. The reason he did not kill him immediately, given his personality, was probably because of him. Because Gyo Cheon had been his master. He was allowing him to resolve the bitterness that still remained.

“You were arrogant in your youth, and you remain arrogant even now.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon understood yet again that his master’s memories had been distorted.

Because the gaze with which the Cult Leader had looked at him in his youth and the gaze he had now were far too different. Time had devoured his master.

“Fine. I will do as you wish.”

Gyo Cheon clapped his hands.

Then the door opened, and an old man entered. He was clearly advanced in years, but his body was sturdy, and his back was straight. A well-formed, muscular physique. If one covered his face and looked only at his body, it would not have been strange to think he was in his twenties or thirties.

Hei Di (黑帝).

It could not be helped that neither Geom Ujin nor the Blood Heaven Blade Demon recognized him.

He was not even a master of the previous generation. He was a master of the generation before that among the Unorthodox Faction, a figure infamous throughout the murim for his cruel nature and outstanding martial arts. In his prime, his martial arts were so excellent that he had been called the greatest master of the Unorthodox Faction.

He committed countless massacres and was named a public enemy of the murim, but in the end, no one managed to kill him.

Since he was well over a hundred years old, everyone naturally thought he had died, but today he had appeared like this.

From the door through which he had emerged, another person walked out.

It was a young woman with heavy makeup. Her red lips were so alluring they seemed capable of tempting anyone.

Qilian Yaoxie (祁連妖邪).

Unlike her youthful appearance, she, too, was a master from the same era as Hei Di, two generations back.

If one were to describe her in a single phrase:

The queen of demonesses.

For well over a hundred years, she had sucked away the internal energy of countless men, and by now she had gone beyond being a demoness and reached the point where she could be called a monster.

What set her apart from other demonesses was the purpose of the vital essence and internal energy she took. She absorbed internal energy not merely to maintain her youth, but to grow stronger, and the result was that she now stood shoulder to shoulder with Hei Di.

Her martial prowess was so formidable that even Hei Di could not carelessly call her a demoness.

Gyo Cheon said proudly,

“They are seniors even to me.”

Though the two old masters had come out knowing their opponent was the Demonic Cult Leader, they showed no fear at all. Of course, that did not mean they were careless. With deep and cautious gazes, they stared at Geom Ujin.

Because they surely knew well the weight that the name Heavenly Demon carried in the murim.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked at Geom Ujin. Geom Ujin, with an expressionless face, seemed to be observing the two, and from his expression, it was impossible to tell what he was thinking.

‘He will probably tell them both to come at him.’

Given the Cult Leader’s personality, he surely would.

But although the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s guess was close to the answer, he did not get it exactly right.

Geom Ujin raised his hand toward one side.

For a moment, it seemed as though no palm force went out.

Then, suddenly, a sound they had never heard before came from the wall.

Fwoooong!

Immediately afterward, the wall where the door through which the two had appeared stood crumbled entirely into powder and collapsed as a whole.

It did not break apart and send fragments flying. Astonishingly, the wall simply turned to powder where it stood and fell.

A cloud of dust rose, revealing the space behind the wall.

There were people waiting there. As though waiting for their own turns, they were seated in different places. The sight was truly bizarre, yet strangely fresh.

Terrifying gleams of light shone from within the dust. From their gazes alone, one could tell. The aura emanating from them was no less than that of Hei Di and Qilian Yaoxie, who had come out before them.

One could tell just by looking at the first to reveal himself.

Kuang Qiansha (狂千殺).

A mad master said to have killed a thousand experts, a god of slaughter, the crazed butcher of the previous generation—Kuang Qiansha.

Kuang Qiansha looked this way and revealed a cold killing intent.

And it was not only him. The gleams of light shining from behind Kuang Qiansha belonged to people who were in no way inferior to this madman. Their number reached over ten. Some faces were familiar, and some were not.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon could tell. They were the very fruits his master had gathered over the past several decades, driven solely by his desire for revenge.

The pressure they exuded filled the hall. Masters of the previous generation, and the generation before that.

Gyo Cheon was staring at Geom Ujin with an expression that seemed to ask what kind of insane stunt this was. Even with just the two who had already come out, it would not have been enough to beg one of them to go back in.

Geom Ujin had called all of them onto the battlefield. He must certainly have read the auras behind the door. The reason he had collapsed the wall was clear.

Demonic energy rose from Geom Ujin’s body and began to flicker around him like flames.

“You lot too. Come all at once.”

Chapter 516: I Want to See More
-------------------------------
It was truly an arrogant thing to say.

Who could possibly tell masters like these to all come at him at once?

And yet no one present dared laugh outright.

Their opponent was the current Cult Leader of the Demon Cult.

The demonic energy rippling around his body was unlike anything they had ever seen. A powerful demonic energy that seemed capable of burning away anything it touched.

Of course, that did not mean the gathered masters were frightened. To begin with, those assembled here were far too formidable. There were no fewer than twelve of them.

Gyo Cheon burst into laughter. It was not the rasping laugh of an old man scraping metal, but a loud, forceful laugh infused with internal energy. He laughed like this deliberately, hoping to break Geom Ujin’s momentum.

“Do you even know who these people are, to spout such arrogance?”

Geom Ujin answered calmly. He was still showing courtesy to him out of consideration for Hyeolcheon Doma. He believed that the only person who could make him withdraw that courtesy was Hyeolcheon Doma himself.

“Are they not the people you gathered?”

The wrinkles at the corners of Gyo Cheon’s eyes twitched. To Gyo Cheon’s ears, those words sounded like, “In the end, are they not merely people who came because you called?”

“Yes, you were arrogant from a young age.”

As he listened to the two speak, Hyeolcheon Doma examined the faces of those who had revealed themselves.

First, there were some whose identities could be known from their appearances alone.

When the wall first collapsed, a man had appeared standing behind Gwangcheon Sal.

His entire face was red. Not merely flushed, but red as though it had been drenched in blood. Among those skilled enough to be here, there was only one man with a face that red.

Jeogan Gongja, the Red-Faced Young Master.

He was a madman who went around saying that so much of his enemies’ blood had splashed onto him that his face had turned red. Despite the title “Young Master,” he was an old monster over seventy.

Another figure stood a little distance away.

His identity could be known from the sword he wore. On the scabbard and hilt were ghostly images painted in dazzling colors.

Gwigeomja, the Ghost Sword.

He was a murderous fiend said to kill without fail whenever his ghost sword cried.

Both of these men possessed skill no less than the Black Emperor or the Qilian Monster who had appeared earlier.

No matter how much his master had used his former status as a Demon Sovereign, and no matter how many long years he had prepared, it could not have been easy to take all of them under his command. They were not the sort to readily place themselves beneath another.

‘What did you promise them?’

Whatever it was, it must have been the reason he was able to gather these masters in one place.

This time, Hyeolcheon Doma looked at Geom Ujin.

From the Cult Leader, he could sense no fear whatsoever.

He was enjoying himself right now. His two eyes, as they gazed at them, were filled with a heat Hyeolcheon Doma had never seen in them before.

This was the Cult Leader as he stood on a battlefield.

He had not knocked down that wall simply to show off his own strength. In the Cult Leader’s battles—no, in his life—there was no such thing as bluff.

Hyeolcheon Doma could guess the Cult Leader’s intention.

It would be easier to face them one by one, but if he did that, this fight would become a war of attrition.

It meant that even if he used internal energy, he would have to spend it in proportion to the number of opponents.

But if he fought them all at once like this, he could kill two with a single move.

Of course, it would be that much more dangerous.

Geom Ujin slowly drew the Heavenly Demon Sword.

The draw was neither fast nor slow.

A strand of sharp energy flowed along the blade of the extended Heavenly Demon Sword, then gleamed at the tip. Then the Heavenly Demon Sword began to cry.

Goooooooooo.

It was like a resonance rising from the depths of the bottomless pit of hell.

At the long-unheard cry of the Heavenly Demon Sword, Hyeolcheon Doma’s heart pounded. He wanted to fight beside the Cult Leader. Just as the Fist Demon and the Cult Leader had fought together in their youth, he too wanted to fight beside him.

But this was the Cult Leader’s battle. There was no place for him to step in.

Goooooooooo.

As the Heavenly Demon Sword cried, Gwangcheon Sal sprang up from his seat. Having lived his entire life being called a mad butcher, he was a man who feared nothing. Even when he heard the Demon Cult Leader tell them all to come at him together, he had remained seated in place as if to make a show of it.

And yet the cry of the Heavenly Demon Sword touched an emotion he had never felt before.

Fear.

He was shocked and bewildered by this unfamiliar emotion. He looked down at his own hand. It was trembling.

‘My hand is shaking just because a sword is crying?’

The fear he felt now was not something his head or his heart was feeling.

After all, he had lived his whole life with the thought, “Life ends in death anyway, so there is nothing to fear.”

This trembling was not the trembling of his heart, but a fear felt by his body.

To hell with the order they had decided on. He wanted to charge forward.

There were two ways to rid oneself of fear. Flee from it, or eliminate the one causing it.

He had always lived by choosing the latter.

And yet his body would not move.

“Damn it!”

Gwangcheon Sal clenched his fists as though they would burst and muttered a low curse. It was a curse directed at both himself and Geom Ujin.

Behind him, Gwigeomja’s sword began to cry with the sound of a ghost.

Hiiiiiiiiii.

In that instant, Gwigeomja felt it. That cry was different from usual.

‘The ghost sword is afraid!’

It was the first time the ghost sword, which had frightened opponents all its life, had ever felt fear.

‘Because of that sword’s cry.’

Gwigeomja began to soothe the ghost sword by pouring his inner force into it.

But there was one person who showed the exact opposite reaction from those two.

A man slowly stepped forward. He was someone who had never once been overwhelmed by another’s spirit in his life. He was the sort who could not stand the sight of anyone giving him orders.

“If I’m told to come out, then I should come out.”

He was a large man, like Magun, and his bare, muscular upper body made him look like a Vajra guardian.

Saekhon Geumgang, the Lust-Soul Vajra.

He was known to possess a body that could not be cut even by sword energy or sword aura. He was a master of monstrous strength, said to shatter boulders and break treasured swords with his bare hands.

With his bare fists, he had gone around crushing the heads of countless martial artists. The reason his infamy was so great was that he had done it simply for amusement.

But the culmination of his evil deeds was the tattoos on his body.

Tiny tattoos were engraved across his enormous frame. Looking closely, they were images of women. Drawings that captured the women’s distinguishing features.

He tattooed the appearances of the women he had violated and killed. When violating another woman, he would show her the tattoos and say this woman had been like this, and that woman had been like that. That was the sort of lunatic he was.

No wonder he said such things without hesitation.

“If the Cult Leader had been a woman, it would have been quite enticing.”

At that moment, another person moved from behind.

“Demon Cult bastards truly have no manners!”

He was an old man whose voice alone conveyed an extraordinary presence.

“Even if your father were here, he would not dare treat me, Seongna Yeomgun, so carelessly. Who are you to flap your mouth, telling me to come out or not?”

He, too, was a master of the previous generation, infamous throughout the martial world. His martial arts were profound, but he was just as cunning and clever.

Even now, while shouting boldly, he had half-positioned himself behind Saekhon Geumgang, cleverly concealing his body.

If Geom Ujin launched a surprise attack, he would hide behind Saekhon Geumgang. As long as Saekhon Geumgang served as his shield, he would not die.

As the two stepped forward, the others also slowly moved and lined up beside them. Their momentum revived as they came out, but Gyo Cheon stopped them.

“Wait! Move according to the plan.”

At those words, Hyeolcheon Doma asked his master,

“Did you even decide the order of deployment?”

Gyo Cheon gave no answer. The one who answered was Saekhon Geumgang.

“No one wanted to go first or last. If you fight him while he’s fresh, it’ll be hard to kill him. And if you go last, you won’t be able to claim the glory of killing the Demon Cult Leader.”

In other words, they believed that although many of them would die, someone here would ultimately kill the Demon Cult Leader.

‘You’re human too. Can you face this much internal energy? In the end, yours will run dry.’

That was their plan for dealing with Geom Ujin.

“So, Demon Cult Leader, stop acting so arrogant and quietly wait your turn to die. It would be even better if you died by my hand, but the unlucky number I drew is eight. Do you think you can last until my turn?”

At Saekhon Geumgang’s shout, Geom Ujin said to him,

“No. You are number two.”

Number two? Not number one, but number two?

Saekhon Geumgang could not understand. If the provocation had angered him, he should have said number one.

At that very instant.

The surroundings suddenly darkened.

The world went dark as though someone had blown out a lamp. Even with the keen eyesight of masters like them, they could not see ahead.

Gyo Cheon quickly took out a luminous pearl and shouted,

“Prepare for a surprise attack!”

Something astonishing happened. The moment the light of the luminous pearl should have illuminated the surroundings, the darkness began to swallow the light again.

‘The luminous pearl doesn’t work?’

A darkness through which no light could pass. It was truly a darkness they were experiencing for the first time.

The next moment, as if nothing had happened, the surroundings brightened. It had occurred so briefly that they wondered if it had merely been an illusion.

Gyo Cheon looked around, but no one was injured or collapsed.

At that moment, Seongna Yeomgun tilted his head and said,

“I heard something.”

Seongna Yeomgun looked around. When it had gone dark, he had clearly heard it. A sharp crack, like ice splitting, had rung beside his ear.

‘What sound was that?’

Then Seongna Yeomgun flinched.

‘Why are they looking at me like that?’

Everyone was looking at him. Their gazes were directed at his body.

Seongna Yeomgun slowly looked down at himself.

In that instant, puzzlement flashed through his eyes.

His body was misaligned.

A single line ran from his right shoulder to his left waist.

His shifted upper body began to slide down along that line.

‘Huh? Huh?’

Srrrrk.

‘I’ve been cut?’

There had been no pain, nor had he felt himself being struck by a sword.

And above all, he had been standing behind Saekhon Geumgang.

He looked at Geom Ujin.

‘How on earth?’

Just as the speed of his falling seemed to increase, his field of vision lowered. The momentary darkness now gifted him eternal darkness. Blood gushing from his body soaked the ground in every direction.

Saekhon Geumgang was just as shocked.

‘I was standing in front.’

Could it be that this single move of the Demon Cult Leader’s could strike beyond an obstacle?

Unfortunately for him, however, this move was not such a technique.

Rip.

A red diagonal line began to be drawn from his shoulder to his side.

His eyes bulged.

‘No way!’

Srrrrk.

His body also began to shift, then soon started sliding down. Both of them had been cut, but because his body was sturdier, the reaction had come more slowly.

It was a body that sword energy and sword aura could not affect.

And his line was not just one.

Rip, riiip, riiiiip.

On his arm, his waist, his shoulder. Red lines began to spread across his entire body like a spiderweb.

“Uaaaaargh!”

With a single monstrous scream, he sprayed blood and collapsed where he stood.

At that sight, not only Gyo Cheon but the other masters wore expressions of disbelief.

They had not fought madly. It had merely gone dark and then bright again.

Then a strange light passed through their eyes as they looked at the corpse.

Everyone’s eyes began to widen.

The reason they were shocked was not because he had killed a man immune to sword energy and sword aura.

Nor was it because he had known Seongna Yeomgun behind him would die first and had called Saekhon Geumgang number two.

Everyone’s gaze turned to the floor.

On his body, now cut into pieces, the images of the women remained intact.

The Demon Cult Leader had not simply carved him apart at random. He had torn his body to pieces while leaving the tattooed women untouched. The women’s faces all remained exactly as they were.

Floating in the blood, they seemed as though they had finally avenged their resentment and found peace.

Hyeolcheon Doma understood. In the Cult Leader’s own way, he had mourned them.

Everyone’s gaze turned toward Geom Ujin.

In that brief instant, within that darkness, without letting the others hear even the slightest sound.

He had killed him without damaging the images of the women.

The Nine Flames Demonic Art, Fourth Form: Dark Flash.

This was Geom Ujin’s Dark Flash, brought to mastery.

The one who broke the heavy silence was Gyo Cheon.

“Did you not expect him to be strong? Every time he uses the Nine Flames Demonic Art, it consumes an enormous amount of internal energy. So do not be afraid!”

It was then. Someone’s voice rang out.

“You should be afraid.”

At the same time, the one who descended from behind was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

When Geom Mugeuk had returned to the safe house with Seo Daeryong, his father had left a message telling him to come here.

Geom Mugeuk had raced over like the wind with his swift footwork, then quietly hidden himself and watched his father’s fight.

After respectfully greeting his father and Hyeolcheon Doma, Geom Mugeuk looked at the enemies and said,

“Why do you think Father broke the wall? Simply to avoid a war of attrition? If that’s what you thought, then you looked down on Father far too much.”

At that, Hyeolcheon Doma flinched. Geom Mugeuk did not miss that expression.

“So that is what you thought, Elder.”

Flustered, Hyeolcheon Doma wore an awkward expression.

“Our rigid elder, who cannot even lie and say he did not!”

Hyeolcheon Doma looked dumbfounded.

‘You rascal, you can joke even in a situation like this?’

Soon, Hyeolcheon Doma smiled faintly. Yes, if anyone would joke in a situation like this, it would be Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned back toward the enemies. Geom Mugeuk knew his father’s intentions exactly.

“Father was trying to calculate. He wanted to assess all of you and decide how to allocate his internal energy. How to kill this one, how to kill that one. He has probably already drawn the entire picture of today’s battle.”

With no playfulness in his face, Geom Mugeuk said to his father,

“I will watch without missing a single movement you make, not even a single breath.”

Others might not know, but at least for him, watching his father fight was worth more than any wondrous opportunity in the martial world.

“So show me more. Father’s battle—I want to see more of it.”

Chapter 517: If You Planned to Mess with the Demonic Cult Leader
----------------------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk and Geom Ujin looked at each other in silence.

In the gaze between them, as if each understood the other's heart without a word, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon envied the Cult Leader.

He had never once envied the Cult Leader before. But now, he found himself thinking that he envied him for being a “father.”

If, as a martial artist, a son said he wanted to see his father fight, would one not want to gladly show him?

Geom Ujin’s gaze turned back toward the enemies. He said nothing, but the cold look in his eyes as he regarded them had already answered in his stead.

Geom Mugeuk also looked at the enemies with him.

Two had died, but there were still ten enemies remaining. And Gyo Cheon besides.

They were not opponents to be taken lightly. More of them were masters of the previous generation than of the current one. On top of that, they were men who had committed all manner of slaughter and evil deeds, and possessed more battle experience than anyone. And there was Gyo Cheon, who had spent his entire life piling up resentment. Even so, Geom Mugeuk did not worry about this.

Could Father perhaps be in danger?

His father was said to possess the greatest martial talent among all the Heavenly Demons in history.

And that father had recently risen to an even higher realm of martial arts. Which meant the power of his Nine Flames Demonic Art would also be different from before the regression. One could tell just from the Dark Flash he had shown earlier.

‘You bastards, this won’t be easy.’

Geom Mugeuk conveyed one piece of news to his father.

“Oh, and the matter with the Orthodox Faction has been settled well. Since the misunderstanding has been cleared up, no matter what happens in Chongqing, they’ll consider it not to be our doing.”

He deliberately spoke loudly for Gyo Cheon to hear. In case the fight turned unfavorable and he tried some useless scheme again.

Gyo Cheon’s expression stiffened, while his father, on the contrary, gave a slight nod with a satisfied look.

The one happier than his father was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Well done.”

The beginning of this matter had been caused by his own master. Before an extreme situation like a Great War between the Orthodox and Demonic paths could erupt, Geom Mugeuk had resolved it well.

At the news Geom Mugeuk delivered, not only Gyo Cheon but the other masters as well sank into an even heavier mood.

Now, no one dared to run their mouth carelessly like the Lust-Soul Vajra or Seongna Yeomgun had.

Gyo Cheon encouraged them to restore their spirits.

“Those two were struck down because of a surprise attack. Do not be shaken!”

But everyone here knew. Even Gyo Cheon, who had shouted, knew.

That move had had nothing to do with a surprise attack.

Even if the Demonic Cult Leader had told them in advance, I will attack now, would they truly have been able to block it?

Having seen Seongna Yeomgun’s and the Lust-Soul Vajra’s bodies sliced apart without either even being able to lift a hand, no one could confidently say they could stop it.

However, as Gyo Cheon had said when they died, each time he used such a tremendous move, the consumption of internal energy would be enormous.

‘We have to make him expend his internal energy.’

And one variable had appeared in this battle.

The appearance of the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader.

Would it be a gain, or a loss?

Viewed negatively, it meant there was one more master they had to kill; viewed positively, it meant there was someone they could take hostage. And not just anyone, but the Demonic Cult Leader’s own blood kin, a hostage of unquestionable value.

They had heard the rumors that the Young Cult Leader’s skill was far from ordinary, but even so, he could not possibly compare to facing that Geom Ujin.

Everyone’s gazes turned toward Giryeon Yosa and the Black Emperor, who had originally been the first to step forward. Those who had been first and second were now third and fourth.

“Let us all attack together!”

At Giryeon Yosa’s words, no one moved.

In truth, she had suggested it, but she had not expected anything. She herself would not have stepped forward either.

The Black Emperor and Giryeon Yosa looked at each other and exchanged glances. Even without exchanging voice transmissions, the two knew how they had to lead this fight.

Pour everything out from the very beginning.

If Geom Ujin used the Nine Flames Demonic Art again, they would die like dogs without even getting to make a move. Before that happened, they had to attack together.

Giryeon Yosa used claw arts, and the Black Emperor used swordsmanship.

The first to move was the Black Emperor. He kicked off the air and rose, immediately releasing sword energy toward Geom Ujin.

What he displayed was Heukcheon Strike, one of the forms he was most confident in among his unique martial art, the Heukcheon Sword Art.

Swaaaek!

It was sword energy like a bolt of lightning descending.

Geom Ujin twisted his body and avoided the sword energy that came crashing down as if to cleave him from above.

Boom!

The descending sword energy left a deep scar in the floor. It was right beside his foot.

Swaaaek! Boom! Swaaaek! Kwaaang!

Heukcheon Strike fell in succession, and Geom Ujin did not block with his sword, instead using footwork to evade. His movements minimized the consumption of internal energy.

Giryeon Yosa attacked at the same time.

Her fingernails had somehow protruded long, as if they had not been trimmed in years, and those sharp, hideous nails were emitting a red glow.

Chwaaaaaak!

What extended from both sets of nails looked like red threads. But they were astral threads called Blood Threads, capable of slicing clean through iron.

She had unleashed Spider Blood Threads, the strongest form of her unique martial art, the Blood Thread Divine Art.

Her Blood Threads were special. They would stretch straight out like spears, then waver like cloth. That was why they were powerful, and at the same time, impossible to predict.

Red lines spread out like a spiderweb and flew toward Geom Ujin.

The Black Emperor’s sword energy and Giryeon Yosa’s Blood Threads.

It was a moment when he had to raise protective astral energy to block, or release sword energy to neutralize their attacks.

But Geom Ujin still used footwork to evade. Like a beast aiming for a single moment, he conserved his internal energy and waited for an opportunity.

Geom Mugeuk did not take his eyes off his father’s fight for even an instant. His father did not fight while being conscious of others. Since his son was watching, he might have thought, Shall I dispose of them impressively? It would have been only natural.

How could he kill all the enemies with the internal energy he had?

His father fought with thorough calculation. If anything, he was a man who could have indulged in every sort of splendor and bravado.

What if such a father were his enemy? Just imagining it already made his chest feel tight.

Papapapapapapak!

Deep traces left by Blood Threads and sword energy remained on the floor and walls.

At first, the two had rejoiced, thinking they had seized the advantage. If the two of them poured out attacks like mad, eventually one would land properly.

But the longer their attacks continued, the more anxious they became.

Geom Ujin found gaps between attacks that should have been impossible to avoid. The attacks were dazzling, and his steps in evasion were calm.

Neither the Black Emperor nor Giryeon Yosa, nor those watching, knew how many times they had shouted this aloud or in their hearts.

That’s it! It hit! We did it!

But it was an illusion, attacks narrowly missing by a hair’s breadth.

Not a single attack touched Geom Ujin’s body.

As he watched the fight, Geom Mugeuk imagined it.

How would he dodge, and how should he respond?

That was why, in his eyes, he saw two figures.

His own image overlapped atop his father’s.

At times he moved exactly like his father, and at times he moved in subtly different ways. And at certain moments, he moved in the opposite direction, only to merge back into one again.

‘I can’t dodge this!’

The version of himself in his imagination, overlapped with his father, drew his sword and struck away the incoming astral energy.

But his father’s movement was different. He avoided the attack by unfolding footwork Geom Mugeuk had not even considered.

‘Ah!’

Each time his father found a path he had not, a tremor ran through Geom Mugeuk’s body. The learning gained from their difference did not simply end with learning something he had not known. Seeing a method he had not known made him think of yet another method.

If only this had not been the situation, he truly wanted to ask his father. What about this method? He would have shared a deep discussion of martial studies with him.

They had intended to make Geom Ujin expend his internal energy, but instead, the two of them were the ones whose internal energy was being consumed severely.

At the end of that anxiety, the Black Emperor unleashed the move he had been saving.

Heukcheon Mugan.

Blue sword energy erupted from the tip of the Black Emperor’s sword, split into eight streams, and flew toward Geom Ujin.

Swaaaaaaaek.

The sword energy poured down, cutting even through Giryeon Yosa’s Blood Threads.

And that was the very moment Geom Ujin had been waiting for.

The instant the Blood Threads were cut away by the Black Emperor’s great attack.

Geom Ujin launched himself forward as if exploding.

Through the severed Blood Threads, Geom Ujin charged. His target was Giryeon Yosa. Heukcheon Mugan should have struck Geom Ujin, but instead, it missed more widely than any attack before it.

Shiiik!

Geom Ujin, who had closed the distance in an instant, slashed Giryeon Yosa.

‘Fast!’

Giryeon Yosa drew her power to its limit and desperately swung both sets of nails.

Paaaak!

But her fingernails were cut away.

Then, without giving her even the time to dodge, Geom Ujin deeply slashed her shoulder, enough for blood to spray in abundance.

At the moment of crisis, the Black Emperor did not help her.

‘Why isn’t he helping?’

He should know very well that if she died, he would be next. Could it be? Had he been frightened and fled?

‘Coward!’

She could not block the third attack.

Puaaak!

With half her waist cut open, she could no longer endure and collapsed on the spot.

Only then could she see. The Black Emperor had not run away; he had been unable to come help her.

Saaaaak.

The Black Emperor was being butchered, surrounded on all sides by someone. What surrounded him were evil spirits.

First Form of the Nine Flames Demonic Art: Annihilation Form.

Geom Ujin’s Annihilation Form had just been unleashed.

When the evil spirits first revealed themselves, the Black Emperor truly felt as if his breath would stop.

They were not merely lumps of astral energy with frightening appearances. A tremendous demonic energy was constricting him from every direction, making his true qi flow poorly.

He unfolded protective astral energy and used every ultimate technique he possessed, but it was no use.

Geom Mugeuk saw it.

They certainly felt larger and more solid than his own evil spirits. The four evil spirits with terrifying faces were hacking the Black Emperor to pieces.

Slice! Slice! Slice! Slice!

It did not end with a single downward strike. They kept striking down as if they were alive. It meant that if the opponent endured the first attack, they could slash him apart a second and third time. It was clearly different from the Annihilation Form he used.

But that was not what had truly changed.

Originally, Geom Ujin’s evil spirits had no expression. Different appearances and emotions were traits unique to Geom Mugeuk’s evil spirits.

But today’s evil spirits were different. Geom Mugeuk sensed some kind of emotion in the eyes of the evil spirits as they looked at his father.

‘It’s different from that time!’

His father’s gaze as he looked at them had also deepened.

An Annihilation Form in actual combat.

It might have been a change and communion that his father, too, was experiencing for the first time.

Soon after, the four evil spirits vanished completely.

In the silence that descended, only the scent of blood grew even thicker.

The surroundings were a mess. Countless traces remained on the floor and walls.

Traces left by Blood Threads, traces left by the Black Emperor’s sword energy.

They were like a spiderweb—no, even denser than a go board.

‘How on earth did he dodge all that?’

To them, those were traces of failure; to Geom Ujin, they were traces of success.

Not even a single hem of Geom Ujin’s clothing had been torn.

And the moment his enemies revealed an opening, for even one instant, he absolutely did not miss that opportunity.

Geom Ujin glanced briefly at Geom Mugeuk.

His father’s eyes seemed to ask, “The fight you said you wanted to see—are you watching it well?”

Geom Mugeuk answered with his eyes. “Yes. I have seen it clearly.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the enemies.

Now that two more had died, eight remained.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk realized something. The enemies who should have numbered eight were seven.

Where did one go?

In that very instant, someone suddenly appeared right before his nose and thrust a sword at him.

Swaaaaaek!

It was none other than the Red-Eyed Young Master, whose entire face had been red.

The technique that had allowed him to move through space in an instant was his trump card, Corridor Extreme Step.

As a user of swift swordsmanship, he possessed an astonishing lightness art. He was certain. Since he had attacked first, his opponent would never be able to block it.

‘If we are to kill the Demonic Cult Leader, we must take his child hostage!’

Because killing was not his objective, his sword flew toward Geom Mugeuk’s shoulder.

He had heard rumors that the Young Cult Leader was extraordinary, but to him, he was still only the Young Cult Leader.

Chaeaaeng!

But his sword was blocked.

Astonishingly, the one who struck away the incoming sword was Geom Ujin.

Faster than Corridor Extreme Step, the lightness art that had arrived in a flashing instant was none other than the Heavenly Demon Flight Art.

“As expected! I knew Father would save me.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s relaxed reaction, a question flashed through the Red-Eyed Young Master’s mind.

‘Surely not? Did he deliberately not dodge because he knew he’d be saved?’

That was an even more astonishing level than dodging, wasn’t it?

But now was not the time to dwell on such thoughts.

The Red-Eyed Young Master swiftly sent his sword flying, launching a surprise attack at Geom Ujin.

Swaaaaaak!

Once again, he struck first.

Chaeng!

But it was blocked again. Geom Ujin’s sword was as fast as his swift sword.

“Thanks to you, I get to watch Father fight from right beside him.”

The moment Geom Mugeuk finished speaking, Geom Ujin’s sword clashed with the Red-Eyed Young Master’s.

Geom Mugeuk did not step back, but watched their battle from where he stood, like a pillar rooted in place.

He did not worry about a stray blade flying at him. With swords moving that fast, if either of them let their attention wander elsewhere, their throat would be pierced by the other’s blade.

Their swords were so fast that it created the illusion of dozens of blades being swung at once. But each of them held only one sword.

Over the Heavenly Demon Sword, the Black Demon Sword seemed to overlap. The two swords moved identically, then diverged, then moved together again.

Even in that moment when swift swords flew back and forth too fast to be seen, both his father’s sword and his own sword were visible in Geom Mugeuk’s mind.

He sensed and learned the difference between the two swords by instinct. It was a speed his mind could not follow.

‘Father, one of these days, I think we’ll have to sit down and talk all night about today’s fight.’

That was how much he felt from watching his father’s battle. And if they did talk through the night, he thought his father would surely gain an achievement from it as well. Because today’s enlightenment was that kind of enlightenment.

As fast as their swords were, the result of the fight came quickly too.

Slice!

With the sound of bone splitting, the battle came to an end.

A result without any upset.

The Red-Eyed Young Master clutched his chest and staggered backward. Blood was flowing out between his fingers.

The Red-Eyed Young Master staggered toward Geom Mugeuk, who was standing beside him.

Because he had tried to take him hostage for nothing and ended up dying, his eyes were filled with resentment.

He seized the hem of Geom Mugeuk’s clothes. Geom Mugeuk let him do as he pleased.

“……You bastard! I should have killed you.”

He sank to his knees, but did not let go of the hem he clutched.

Geom Mugeuk looked down into his eyes, where life was fading away.

Then he wiped the blood from the man’s face with his hand and smeared it in red streaks across his own face.

“From now on, I’m the Red-Eyed Young Master. You’re old as dirt—what kind of young master are you?”

Glaring at him with a face full of rage, the Red-Eyed Young Master died then and there.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Gyo Cheon and grinned broadly with his blood-smeared face.

“I’m your Red-Eyed Young Master now, so don’t even think of taking me hostage.”

Anger and absurdity flashed across Gyo Cheon’s face at the same time.

“Madman.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze toward him sank coldly.

“If you meant to lay a hand on the Demonic Cult Leader, you should have come even madder than me.”

Chapter 518: I Have Only One Master-Disciple Relationship
---------------------------------------------------------
At Geom Mugeuk’s cold gaze, Gyocheon felt a chill in his chest. He was the Young Cult Leader, someone who should have been laughable to him. Perhaps it was because his words had struck home.

‘I should have come even madder?’

Looking at Geom Ujin standing vividly among those who had become corpses, he thought that perhaps he really should have.

Just as the Geom Ujin he had investigated was different from reality, that Young Cult Leader too felt different from the one he had investigated.

He knew it had been buried like a jest, but that figure looking at him, its face covered in blood, sent a cold wind blowing through his chest.

Gyocheon’s gaze naturally turned to Geom Ujin beside him.

‘Like father, like son, is that it?’

Meanwhile, Geom Ujin was looking at his son, whose face was stained with blood, with an expression of pity. Having only ever seen a stern Geom Ujin, this sight felt slightly unfamiliar.

Gyocheon did not know. That this change was precisely what occupied the greatest weight among the many reasons Geom Ujin had grown stronger. Because he did not know the process, only the result surprised him.

‘He is far stronger than the previous Cult Leader.’

How on earth? He knew better than anyone how difficult it was for martial artists of Cult Leader caliber to raise their martial prowess.

Gyocheon hid such doubts with effort and looked at the remaining experts.

Of the twelve experts, five were dead. Now seven remained.

“Whose turn is next?”

It was the will to push through the original plan to somehow exhaust his internal energy and kill him.

At that sight, Hyeolcheon Doma sighed inwardly.

His master was rigid. When the situation changed, the plan should change too. But his frozen mind thought only of pushing forward.

‘Will these men accomplish what the previous five could not?’

It was a moment when anyone would have hesitated, but one man strode out.

He was Gwangcheonsal. He was precisely the one who had first felt terror at the cry of the Cheonmageom earlier.

Yet now, his eyes overflowed with confidence.

He was one who had spent his life killing people like a madman starving for blood. At this moment, he realized that only that madness could save him.

‘Fear is your lot, not mine.’

Another person stepped forth beside Gwangcheonsal. It was Gwi Geomja, who had earlier been flustered when his ghost sword cried out in fear.

Gwangcheonsal and Gwi Geomja.

They had been symbols of terror in their respective domains.

His son had come, so it would have been natural to tell him to take one of them.

But Geom Ujin walked forward as if to say he would face them all himself.

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. That today’s battle was an important one for his father as well. That his father was settling that day.

All while being considerate of Hyeolcheon Doma’s heart.

Rather, it was a good opportunity for Geom Mugeuk.

He intended to commit the entirety of this battle, from beginning to end, to memory. So that, like replaying a match of Go, he could chew it over again and again to add to his own martial prowess.

Gwangcheonsal donned gauntlets made of steel on both fists.

His beloved weapon, the Lang Ya Tie Quan.

True to its name, sharp blades protruded from each knuckle like the fangs of a wolf. Judging by the flowing ethereal energy, it was not made of ordinary metal.

“With these fists, I have beaten to death all who offended my eyes!”

His voice, infused with internal energy, rang out loud and clear.

When he wore the Lang Ya Tie Quan, he changed. Because his fists remembered. All those countless people he had killed.

‘I can win! I’ll kill the Cult Leader and chew him up alive!’

Madness erupted from his eyes.

Geom Ujin, who had been silently watching him, sheathed the Cheonmageom.

Then he gestured for him to come closer.

A challenge to fight with bare fists.

Gwangcheonsal smiled with confidence. If it was Lang Ya Tie Quan against bare hands, he was sure he could stand a chance even against the Cult Leader.

Huaaaaaang!

His steel fist flew toward Geom Ujin with tremendous force.

Kuang Ren Sha Ji.

It was the move he was most confident in.

Geom Ujin likewise threw a fist and charged.

Fist against fist.

Gwangcheonsal poured every ounce of his internal energy into this fist.

‘The one shattering will be your fist!’

Gyocheon hoped. Even if that blow could not kill Geom Ujin, he wished it would inflict internal injuries. That it would exhaust his internal energy as much as possible.

But Geom Ujin absolutely never fought the way his opponent predicted.

Just before fist clashed against fist, at that final moment, Geom Ujin twisted his body.

The instant the fist carrying tremendous force grazed his face, Geom Ujin had already burrowed into Gwangcheonsal’s chest.

‘Damn it!’

He had never imagined the Heavenly Demon would avoid a frontal clash, and Geom Ujin’s movement—evading and penetrating—was too fast and exquisite.

Gwangcheonsal raised his protective astral energy over his entire body, preparing for Geom Ujin’s blow.

But no powerful blow came flying.

The very instant their gazes met!

Tap.

Geom Ujin lightly struck Gwangcheonsal’s head. An act that seemed to say, ‘Get a grip, you bastard!’

Gwangcheonsal’s face twisted viciously.

‘You dare mock me?’

Even if he had been struck hard, he would never have fallen. His protective astral energy had been guarding even his head perfectly.

Or so he had thought.

“Kghhh!”

Gwangcheonsal let out a bizarre scream as he tried to launch a second attack and toppled backward.

With his eyes rolled back white, he was already dead. His outward appearance was intact. There were no wounds, no bleeding.

He died without ever knowing.

That Geom Ujin was not someone who simply tapped his opponent’s head in a fight. That if he tapped it, there was a clear reason.

That blow, light as a tap, gently penetrated the opponent’s protective astral energy and, only later, became a great shockwave that completely overturned his brain.

It was Geom Ujin’s Nei Jia Zhong Shou Fa, a martial art that injured the interior by striking the exterior, pushed to its extreme.

Not only Gwi Geomja, who had stepped out together, but Gyocheon and the other experts—all their faces were filled with shock.

They would have been less surprised if Geom Ujin’s fist had shattered the iron gauntlets.

Just as when he had killed the previous five, Geom Ujin did not show off his strength. He focused solely on how to kill his enemies most efficiently. He was an absolute being who did not let his guard down, an absolute being who used his head.

He used the Jiu Hua Mo Gong boldly when he had to, and killed with so little internal energy like this as well. While completely immersed in his own fight, on the other hand, it seemed as though he were showing his son.

My son, if you must fight twelve absolute experts in a Wheel Battle, fight like this.

The ghost sword in Gwi Geomja’s hand began to wail like a ghost’s cry. A mournful, eerie wail, as though foreseeing someone’s fate.

He had not merely been watching while Gwangcheonsal died.

He had been preparing to unleash his supreme ultimate technique by raising his internal energy. Because he had seen again and again that if he did not do so on the first move, he would never get to unleash it.

Chwaaaaang!

Jet-black ghostly energy burst from his sword and poured down upon Geom Ujin.

Gui Jian Hun Yuan Zhan!

The ghostly energy whipped around Geom Ujin like a tornado.

It looked as though a giant black serpent would coil around Geom Ujin’s body and burst him apart.

‘It worked!’

As the first move landed, Gwi Geomja raised his internal energy to the extreme.

‘Cult Leader! Burst and die just like this!’

The swirling black energy tightened further around Geom Ujin, and his figure could no longer be seen.

Expectation flashed in the eyes of Gyocheon and the other experts.

‘Even if it doesn’t kill him, he’ll suffer great internal injuries!’

It was precisely then.

Sssssssk!

Light seeped through the black tornado.

As though the sea parted, the black energy split, and something shot out.

It was the Cheonmageom, shimmering with pure white demonic energy.

At that sight, the ghost sword wailed even louder. It was a cry of astonishment and shock reflecting its master’s heart.

The next moment, a rain of sword energy poured from the sky.

Jiu Hua Mo Gong, Fifth Form: Jue Hun Mo Ji.

Because it had attacked as though giving warning, Gwi Geomja threw his body aside and instinctively dodged the sword energy falling toward the crown of his head.

‘I dodged it!’

But his joy was short-lived. The falling sword energy was not a single strike.

Puak!

Another blade of sword energy falling from the sky pierced his shoulder.

Puaaak!

A following strike impaled the crown of his head.

Over his body, which had collapsed dead on the spot, sword energy continued to fall in intervals.

Puuk! Puuk!

Originally, the Jue Hun Mo Ji was an attack that simultaneously rained sword energy down upon the crowns of several heads. He had even trained together with Geom Mugeuk to rescue hostages with it.

This time, that attack was concentrated solely around Gwi Geomja. With delays between strikes so that it was absolutely impossible to dodge.

Geom Mugeuk understood.

The decisive difference from his own Jue Hun Mo Ji was neither the number of falling sword energies nor their power.

The intervals.

His father’s Jue Hun Mo Ji predicted the situation in which the opponent would dodge and fell with intervals.

Geom Mugeuk knew better than anyone how difficult a realm this was.

‘Could I truly evade my father’s Jue Hun Mo Ji?’

Geom Mugeuk could not dare guarantee it.

‘It is a realm I must definitely achieve. No, I must ascend to a realm even higher than this.’

For if he ascended, his father would ascend as well.

When Gwi Geomja collapsed in a mangled heap, the black tornado swirling around Geom Ujin vanished in an instant.

Gyocheon’s face hardened even further.

No matter how strong, this was absurd. Gwangcheonsal and Gwi Geomja dying in a single move each? What plan could one possibly devise against such an opponent? The words that rose in his mind now were not “greatest under heaven,” but “greatest in history.”

Geom Ujin asked him.

“Will you step forward yourself now?”

Geom Ujin still treated him with courtesy. Because Hyeolcheon Doma still considered him his master.

To his master, who was glaring at Geom Ujin in silence, Hyeolcheon Doma spoke.

“It is not too late even now.”

At that, Gyocheon snorted, looking at those who had become corpses.

“You say that even after seeing them?”

“The dead are the dead, and the living must live. Even now, admit your wrongs to the Cult Leader.”

Gyocheon felt it. That unlike the Cult Leader or the Young Cult Leader, only his disciple wished somehow for him to survive.

Yet even feeling that, kind words did not leave his mouth.

“You traitor, pretending to care for me before the Cult Leader.”

Hyeolcheon Doma made no excuses for him. Geom Ujin merely watched him in silence.

It was Geom Mugeuk who took his place.

“That is enough.”

Everyone’s gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk. At first, they thought he was speaking to Gyocheon—to tell him to stop. But those words were directed not at Gyocheon, but at Hyeolcheon Doma.

“You have done enough, Elder.”

Since the Cult Leader was present, Hyeolcheon Doma replied courteously.

“Young Cult Leader, please do not interfere.”

“No, this is a problem the parties themselves cannot resolve.”

Geom Mugeuk knew. If Gyocheon died again today, it would once again remain as a deep wound in Hyeolcheon Doma’s heart. For it would mean his master dying before his eyes a second time.

“Your master will pretend until the end that he does not know your heart. Only then will this act he has committed gain justification.”

At that, Gyocheon shouted furiously.

“Silence! What do you know!”

Compared to the agitated Gyocheon, Geom Mugeuk was calm.

“You keep speaking of betrayal, but by that logic, did you not also betray the main cult? If your disciple betrayed you, you should have sought revenge on the disciple alone; why did you drag my father into it? Why did you seek to tarnish the cult’s honor and start a war with the orthodox faction? Why did you drag even your own disciple into it?”

Battered by this torrent of rebukes, Gyocheon could not immediately retort, and Geom Mugeuk’s offensive continued.

“To be honest, your disciple’s betrayal is not what matters to you, is it? Rather, you thought it worked out well—since it has come to this, let us seize the entire martial world. Is that not the heart with which you have been expanding your forces?”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to the remaining experts.

“What did you promise them? That you would swallow up both the orthodox and unorthodox factions through sowing discord, and what would you give them?”

The moment he heard those words, Hyeolcheon Doma felt it. That his master might truly have harbored such intentions. That the scheme to sow discord with the orthodox faction might not have been merely for revenge. That he might have gathered the ambitions of these old villains to fulfill a dream he had never achieved.

“So stop using betrayal as an excuse now.”

Geom Mugeuk actually did not know well what was in his heart. Nor did he wish to know.

Yet the reason he drove him into a corner was not to attack him. It was for Hyeolcheon Doma.

He was telling him not to dwell on the past. That his master was not a man worth such regard.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Hyeolcheon Doma and spoke.

“Are you sorry to your Master? No, the one you should be sorry to is not your Master, but your disciple, Elder.”

“!”

“Because he took the risk alone for the sake of this worthless master-disciple bond. For such a Master, your disciple came here alone. That fearful and timid disciple of yours.”

Hyeolcheon Doma’s pupils trembled. Preoccupied with his master and the Cult Leader, he had not been able to pay any attention to Seo Daeryong.

“If this is the master-disciple bond to which you should attach meaning, do you still remain here?”

Hyeolcheon Doma turned his head and looked at Gyocheon.

Gyocheon looked at him coldly, but Hyeolcheon Doma’s gaze as he looked back was still burning.

After gazing at each other like that for a long while, he finally conveyed his last sentiments.

“You are right. It was not because of my Master, but to lessen my own guilt that I did it. That is why I wanted to save you. That is why…… I am even more sorry.”

Gyocheon’s gaze flickered.

“I will see you off on your final journey. That guilt, I will carry it for the rest of my life. It was trying to cast aside what I should have carried that caused the problem, was it not.”

As if it were a final farewell in his capacity as a master, he bowed politely with propriety.

Only then did he answer Geom Mugeuk’s question.

“The place where I must attach meaning is not here.”

Though his earlier words had trembled, the words that followed were resolute.

“From now on, I have only one master-disciple relationship.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Hyeolcheon Doma and smiled brightly.

‘Elder!’

There was a reason he had pushed him like this.

Because Gyocheon had to hear these words before he died. After Gyocheon died, Hyeolcheon Doma would never have been able to say what he had just said. A fight with a dead person is the most difficult fight in the world.

Even someone this smart and wise, once entangled in wounds of the past, cannot easily escape from them.

Past wounds have nothing to do with present intelligence. No, rather, the smarter one is, the harder it may be to break free.

Hyeolcheon Doma too looked at Geom Mugeuk and smiled faintly.

‘Thank you.’

‘Elder, hurry back and sit side by side reading books at that sunny windowsill.’

Geom Woojin looked at Geom Mugeuk and nodded slightly.

It was evident from his expression. That his father had praised him for doing well. His father too had hoped that no emotional wound would remain in Hyeolcheon Doma.

Now with a refreshed expression, Geom Woojin turned toward the enemy.

He locked eyes with one white-haired old man among the remaining five experts.

“It has been a while.”

Then the old man asked with a surprised face.

“Do you remember me?”

Geom Woojin nodded.

“What a remarkable memory. I saw you when you were very young.”

Surprisingly, he was a Demon of the Heavenly Demon Cult.

Honma (Mixed Demon).

The old man was a retired Demon of the Heavenly Demon Cult. Possessing great skill, he had held several key positions within the cult. And he had been a man with deep ties to Gyocheon at the time.

“I had no choice because of an urgent request.”

Geom Woojin asked bluntly.

“Did you know this was a scheme targeting me?”

After a brief pause, Honma answered.

“I knew.”

Geom Woojin no longer tried to converse with him. Because this betrayal was far worse in nature than Gyocheon’s.

“Then there was no need for you to step forth.”

As Hyeolcheon Doma settled his heart, Geom Woojin too began to clean up in earnest.

A fiend silently revealed itself before Geom Woojin.

It was a fiend with a more terrifying and more intense impression than the fiend of the Annihilation Rite shown earlier.

Sssssssh.

The fiend began to divide.

This was precisely the reason he had said there was no need to come out.

“Die together there.”

Chapter 519: The One in Father’s Heart.
---------------------------------------
It was Geom Ujin’s Great Annihilation Form.

His father’s evil spirits finished dividing in an instant.

The moment Geom Mugeuk saw them, he knew.

‘It’s changed.’

Once before, his father had shown him the Great Annihilation Form. That day, his father’s Great Annihilation Form had clashed with his own.

Forty-four against four.

That day, his own evil spirits had been annihilated by his father’s. He had raised even his protective astral qi and resisted to the very end.

His evil spirits were far stronger now than they had been then, but the same was true of his father. His father’s evil spirits were larger than before, and looked far more powerful. Just from the demonic qi they emitted, he could tell they had grown stronger.

‘Father’s Nine Flames Demonic Art has surpassed the tenth-star great completion and is heading toward a new realm!’

Geom Mugeuk said to his own evil spirits.

‘You’ll have to eat more and grow bigger.’

If that was how it was, then what pressure must Honma and the remaining masters have felt?

Wooooooo.

With a low resonance, the demonic qi exuded by the evil spirits lined up before them sent chills crawling over their entire bodies.

Honma sensed that they could never block the martial art unfolding before them.

“It’s the Nine Flames Demonic Art! Toward the Young Cult Leader!”

Honma desperately threw himself forward. The four remaining masters moved with him.

They could not escape by breaking through the walls or ceiling.

When the Great Annihilation Form was unleashed, the powerful demonic qi emitted by the manifested evil spirits took control of the space. As if trapped behind invisible walls, they could not leave the domain where the evil spirits stood in formation.

Since Honma and the four masters had changed position, Geom Mugeuk expected his father to stop the Great Annihilation Form and unleash it again in another direction.

Then something utterly unexpected happened.

Sssssss.

The evil spirits standing in formation changed direction. At the sight, Geom Mugeuk was startled. The Great Annihilation Form he used only divided and then immediately pressed forward.

But in his father’s Great Annihilation Form, the evil spirits changed direction.

Geom Mugeuk realized that the reason his father had not simply swept them away before they dodged was to show him this sight.

If it had already changed this much, he truly could not imagine what the Nine Flames Demonic Art would look like if it reached the twelfth-star great completion.

Of course, even at this moment, an enormous amount of internal energy must have been consumed.

Staring at the swords of the evil spirits thrust toward him, Honma felt his whole body freeze. His gaze turned toward Gyo Cheon in the distance.

—Are you just going to watch like this? Use what you prepared.

Even after the sound transmission reached him, Gyo Cheon only watched the situation without answering.

From his reaction, Honma understood. From the very beginning, Gyo Cheon had intended to use them all as expendable pieces. After making the Cult Leader consume as much internal energy as possible, he had planned to finish things himself.

Gyo Cheon had come to find him when he had retired and was living alone. He had thought the man dead, and the first words Gyo Cheon said upon seeing him were these.

—Let us take this murim together.

When he heard those words, he should have rejected him outright, asking what kind of madness that was. He should have said that, out of consideration for their old friendship, he would not report him to the cult, so he should leave at once.

—Why are we not allowed to dream such dreams? Why do such grand dreams belong only to them?

Those words had made his heart stir. He had thought this: if he made good use of this man’s desire for revenge, perhaps he could spend the final years of his life splendidly.

A life lived only once, in any case.

A life already lived long enough, in any case.

He had been unable to resist that powerful temptation. At the time of his retirement, he had been living a life far too idle and tedious.

Desire, at an age when it should have flowed away like water, burned his body like fire.

And now, as Honma looked at the evil spirits lined up before him, he awakened from the dream.

‘I thought I was using him, but in the end, I was the one being used.’

For Gyo Cheon, revenge had been everything. Even if it meant sacrificing everyone, his goal must have been to make the Cult Leader expend his internal energy and then step forward at the end to take his revenge.

Surely, after preparing this much, he would not end with mere revenge? After gathering these people with such difficulty?

Yet it seemed that was exactly what he had done. He had merely been an incarnation of revenge who had scraped together the desires of old men.

Only now, at this moment, did Honma realize it.

Now, the only thing he could believe in was one thing.

“Cult Leader, please set aside your anger. You do not intend to unleash the Nine Flames Demonic Art with your son here, do you?”

Geom Ujin stared at Honma with an unreadable expression.

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke from behind him.

“That means you don’t know how heartless my father can be.”

Ignoring Geom Mugeuk’s words, Honma said to everyone,

“As long as the Young Cult Leader is here, he absolutely won’t be able to release it.”

Half of the four masters guarded their rear, preparing for Geom Mugeuk and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon to attack them.

Thud.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon planted the Heaven-Destroying Greatsaber in front of Geom Mugeuk.

“It won’t block this either, but it should help a little.”

Geom Mugeuk said to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon,

“As expected, Elder, you know my father far too well.”

It meant he believed Geom Ujin would unleash it even with his son there.

At the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s reaction, Honma grew even more desperate.

“Cult Leader, withdraw the Nine Flames Demonic Art. If we attack together at once, the Young Cult Leader will not survive. Think of your son!”

But his opponent was not someone threats would work on.

The moment the corners of Geom Ujin’s lips lifted slightly, the Great Annihilation Form was unleashed.

Kwakakakakakakakakak!

The evil spirits surged forward all at once.

Honma and the remaining masters raised their protective astral qi and released their aura.

Swaaaek! Swaek! Swaaaeeek!

Puh-uhk! Kwaang! Kwang! Kwang!

The torrents of aura collided with the evil spirits. Their attacks held enough power to turn boulders into powder.

But the evil spirits only staggered once and, without being annihilated, pushed straight in.

“No!”

An evil spirit struck Honma with overwhelming force. He could never block this powerful mass of aura created by the Nine Flames Demonic Art.

Puhpuhpuhpuk!

Honma’s bones and flesh shattered and scattered in every direction.

The other four masters met the same result, with only slight differences in timing.

They swung their swords, but the blades broke. They endured with protective astral qi, but were torn to shreds. They tried to flee, but were dragged in by the demonic qi and smashed to pieces.

Kwakakakakakakakaka!

Having swept them all away, the evil spirits surged toward Geom Mugeuk and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon behind them.

Geom Mugeuk stood still and watched the evil spirits of the Great Annihilation Form rushing toward him.

The terrifying evil spirits were crashing in like a tidal wave. The demonic qi they emitted was so intense that even he, who cultivated demonic arts, found it hard to breathe.

‘So this is what it feels like to be on the receiving end of the Great Annihilation Form.’

Kwaaaaaaaa!

The surging evil spirits broke through the entire wall behind where the two of them had been standing.

One evil spirit came to a stop.

An evil spirit had stopped before Geom Mugeuk and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

It was larger than even the Heaven-Destroying Greatsaber planted before them. His father had stopped only the evil spirit in the place where his son and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon stood.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked at the evil spirit and respectfully cupped his fist, paying his respects to the Nine Flames Demonic Art.

Geom Mugeuk silently stared at the evil spirit before him.

The evil spirit stared straight ahead with eyes both terrifying and cold. It looked vivid, as if it had just emerged from hell.

Originally, he had intended to raise the Great Demon Wall. But at the final moment, he changed his mind. Surely his father would not kill his own son? He trusted his father.

Sssss.

The evil spirit that had stood so proudly slowly disappeared. Just before it vanished, he had the illusion that it lowered its eyes and gave him a sidelong glance.

Only after the evil spirit disappeared did the scene before his eyes fully reveal itself.

In the silence left behind by death, blood that had not yet cooled was flowing.

The twelve masters who had been here had all been killed by Geom Ujin. Now it was impossible to tell how many there had originally been.

The only one left was Gyo Cheon.

“Kuhahahahaha!”

Gyo Cheon laughed. His reflection in the pool of blood looked like a madman. He looked like someone who had given up everything, and also like someone who had known it would turn out this way.

Geom Ujin’s gaze turned toward Gyo Cheon. Geom Mugeuk and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon behind him also looked at him.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon shouted at Gyo Cheon,

“Did you truly not know it would end like this?”

Gyo Cheon abruptly stopped laughing and looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon with sunken eyes. His gaze seemed to hold something he wanted to say, but he said nothing.

Then Geom Ujin turned to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and said,

“Leave it to me to the end.”

Geom Mugeuk understood. It was not merely because of what Gyo Cheon had prepared as his final move, or how dangerous it was, but because his father did not want the Blood Heaven Blade Demon to suffer the fate of having to kill his master again.

Even though he did not show it outwardly, Geom Mugeuk could feel that his father cared for the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Geom Ujin slowly walked toward Gyo Cheon, his sword hanging low.

Even at the very end, he thought of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. He intended to kill Gyo Cheon in a way that would leave his corpse intact.

“Do you have no words to leave your disciple?”

Gyo Cheon glanced at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and snorted.

Then, with a meaningful gaze, he asked in return,

“And you? Do you have no words to leave your son?”

The moment he finished speaking.

Puk!

Gyo Cheon’s form turned to smoke and disappeared.

At the same time, everything around them began to vanish.

The walls, the tables, the chairs, and the corpses on the floor.

Puk! Puk! Puk! Puk!

Even Geom Mugeuk and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. All of them scattered into smoke. As though everything until now had been an illusion.

Geom Ujin said quietly,

“Illusion arts!”

When everything vanished, they were deep in the mountains. Now that he looked, this place was? This was Daecheon Mountain, where the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was located.

Geom Ujin understood. It was the place where he had gone hunting that day.

Not only his surroundings had changed; he himself had changed as well. The sword at his waist was not the Heavenly Demon Sword. He drew it and reflected his face on the blade, and saw himself in his youth. The version of himself before he had learned the Nine Flames Demonic Art.

It was at that very moment.

“Ujin.”

Startled, Geom Ujin turned around, and saw his elder brother from his youth standing some distance away.

Geom Ujin’s gaze trembled as he looked at him. His brother truly stood there in the form he had worn that day. Back then, he had hated him so much. Yet even as the years passed, his brother had never vanished from his life. Rather, the traces of his brother had only grown deeper.

His brother spoke the exact words he had said to him that day.

“You knew it would come to this anyway, didn’t you?”

A cold killing intent flowed from his brother. That day, even though he had known in advance, he had still been enraged when he saw his brother actually trying to kill him. But today, his feelings upon seeing his brother were different.

“At the time, I thought so.”

And for the first time, Geom Ujin let out a sigh. Like the self of that era, who had shown his emotions openly, Geom Ujin revealed his regret.

“There must have been another way, Brother.”

It would not have been an easy path, but there surely must have been a way. His son had shown him as much by personally accomplishing that difficult feat.

“There is no other path. Neither you nor I would ever have yielded.”

Even so, they should have searched for a way. His younger self back then had not been able to find it. What about now? Now that he had watched everything his son had accomplished?

It was then that someone appeared behind him.

“Second Young Master, I regret that things have come to this.”

It was Gyo Cheon, holding a greatsaber. Not the collapsed, aged form of the present, but the Blade Demon of those days, when he had burned with ambition.

However, as if this was not a perfect recreation of that day, Gyo Cheon spoke with a meaningful look.

“That day, I failed to kill you because of my disciple’s betrayal. But today will be different.”

Killing intent swirled in Gyo Cheon’s eyes. That day, too, he had tried to kill him like this.

Because both he and Geom Ujin remembered that day, this illusion art must have been able to manifest it so vividly.

Then a voice came from behind him.

“Today certainly is different.”

Another person appeared there.

“Because instead of your disciple, your son has come.”

Astonishingly, the one who appeared was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

“Perhaps because of the Nine Flames Demonic Art, I entered this illusion as well.”

The father of his youth and the son stood facing each other.

The two looked at one another with heated gazes.

“To see you in your youth, Father, truly moves me.”

Geom Ujin’s eyes, as he looked silently at his son, were deeper than anything.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward Geom Ujin’s brother.

“So that is what my uncle looked like.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at him, respectfully cupped his fist, and greeted him.

“This is the first time I am greeting you. I am Mugeuk.”

He glared at Geom Mugeuk with a displeased expression. His reaction was vivid, as if he were a real person. Geom Mugeuk asked his father,

“What kind of illusion art have we fallen into?”

Geom Ujin knew exactly what kind of illusion art he had fallen into.

“It is an illusion art called the Phantom Heart Demon Art. It is a martial art that uses the person who occupies the largest and deepest place in the target’s heart to kill the one caught in it.”

In other words, it could be the person one hated the most, or the person one liked and loved the most.

“The person who occupied the largest place in your heart was your brother, Father.”

Most illusion arts belonged to the unorthodox factions, but this Phantom Heart Demon Art was a martial art passed down through the orthodox factions. Since all demonic arts were rendered powerless before the supreme demonic art, the Nine Flames Demonic Art, Gyo Cheon had used an orthodox martial art instead. He must have trained his entire life to finish his revenge with the Phantom Heart Demon Art.

“But he failed to account for the variable that is me.”

Geom Mugeuk drew his sword and walked to Geom Ujin’s side.

“Father, I know you may regret it, but let us forget the past now.”

Geom Ujin nodded and walked forward.

Step, step.

He moved toward his brother. Within the illusion, only the sound of his footsteps could be heard.

His brother also drew his sword, just as he had that day, and walked forward.

Beside him, Gyo Cheon walked forward as well.

The moment Geom Ujin faced both of them at once, something unimaginable happened.

Geom Ujin thrust his sword with speed like light.

Shiiiiik.

Puuuk!

The one whose chest was pierced through, who coughed up a surge of blood, was Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Ujin had turned around with lightning speed and stabbed his son behind him.

“……Father?”

Chapter 520: A Man Who Lived with Misfortune.
---------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk stared at Geom Ujin, eyes wide.

The gaze with which Geom Ujin looked upon his son was cold as ice.

“……Why me?”

*Thuk.*

But the sword embedded itself even deeper into Geom Mugeuk. Blood dripped from the blade protruding from his back.

“If you had known my son well, this is a move you might have fallen for.”

Then, blue light began to flow from the wound where the blade was lodged.

With it began fragmentation.

*Screech.*

Cracks began to form on Geom Mugeuk’s face. They gradually spread until they branched across his face and neck, arms and body like a spiderweb.

*Swoosh!*

In the instant blue light poured out all at once from the cracked places, the fractured pieces came tumbling down with a sharp *ching*.

The one who revealed himself from within was Gyocheon. Dense wrinkles took the place of the spiderweb-like cracks.

*Sssssss.*

Following that, Geom Ujin returned to his true form. The appearance of the young Geom Ujin vanished and he returned to his original self. The sword that had pierced Gyocheon also changed into the Cheonma Sword.

*Hack.*

Blood burst from Gyocheon’s mouth with a cough.

In that instant, the trees surrounding them disappeared.

*Cough, cough.*

With the continuing coughs, this time the rocks, birds, and sky disappeared. The Gyocheon of that time, who had halted his attack from behind, disappeared.

And Geom Ujin’s older brother began to disappear.

Then Geom Ujin placed his palm on Gyocheon’s chest and infused a stream of internal energy.

As Gyocheon’s face eased slightly, the older brother who had been about to disappear stood firmly in place.

Geom Ujin silently looked at his older brother.

Even though it was an older brother from within the illusion technique born of Gyocheon’s memories and his own heart.

Although he had already changed from his youthful appearance back to his true form, a wistful expression reminiscent of his younger days settled upon Geom Ujin’s face.

“Keu-e-e-ek.”

Gyocheon spat out the blood he had been holding back, and the older brother vanished.

Geom Ujin watched his older brother disappear with a deep gaze.

And so, as the illusion technique shattered, everything around them disappeared.

Geom Ujin and Gyocheon stood in the place where they had originally been.

In their original places stood Geom Mugeuk, Hyeolcheondoma, and Hwi.

Moments ago, when Geom Ujin suddenly vanished, they had known he had fallen into Gyocheon’s sorcery.

Hwi had urgently revealed himself, but Geom Ujin had already disappeared. No matter how much he believed in the Cult Lord’s strength, he could not help but worry because he was a bodyguard.

Then Geom Mugeuk said this to Hwi:

“No one can harm Father as he is now.”

Nevertheless, because they knew it was Gyocheon’s final move, the three of them waited for their father with tense hearts.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk did not forget a jest.

“No matter how hidden a trump card it is, who would want to be alone with Father?”

At his words, Hyeolcheondoma and Hwi smiled faintly.

And now, the Cult Lord had appeared boldly, meeting their expectations.

Bloody froth gushed from Gyocheon’s mouth.

“……How did you know it was me?”

He had believed that disguising himself as Geom Mugeuk in that situation would make it impossible to tell.

The method to break this Illusionary Heart Demon Technique was Geom Mugeuk himself.

Moreover, their relationship was that of father and son. He had believed it would be impossible to find him. Yet the moment he tried to ambush from behind, Geom Ujin had stabbed him first.

Geom Ujin spoke to him with calm eyes.

“Did I not say earlier that I know what this Illusionary Heart Demon Technique is?”

That it kills by using the person who occupies the largest, deepest place in one’s heart.

“I thought… it would be your older brother.”

He had thought Geom Ujin buried his older brother deepest and largest in his heart.

Gyocheon’s gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk.

“……So it was his son who occupied the largest, deepest place in the Cult Lord’s heart.”

And more than anything, Geom Ujin knew this fact himself. That was why he could thrust his sword into his son’s chest with certainty.

The moment he heard Gyocheon’s words, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes trembled. Before joy, he was surprised and bewildered.
