*The person who occupies the largest place in Father’s heart is me?*

Undoubtedly, his father occupied the largest place in his own heart. But he had never expected that he would be the largest in his father’s.

*Father!*

Since regressing, he had felt many emotions toward his father, but he had never been as happy as he was in this moment.

Meanwhile, his father stared at Gyocheon without revealing any emotion.

Thinking things would become awkward if they turned serious now, Geom Mugeuk cracked a joke.

“Of course it’s me! If you drew Father’s heart as a picture, this second son would take up slightly more than half. Then of the remaining half, the Cult would take half again. And from the remainder, a little for my older brother, a little for the Demon Venerables, a little for the Nine Transformations Demonic Art, a little for Father’s dreams, and a little for fishing. That’s how it would be.”

A smile formed at Geom Ujin’s lips. Since he had his back turned to Geom Mugeuk, the latter could not see it.

Geom Ujin added to Gyocheon:

“If it had really been that boy, he would have said ten times more than what you said earlier. And he wouldn’t have said to pull out the sword and kill the opponent to quickly forget the past. He probably would have had… things to say to my older brother as well.”

Hyeolcheondoma could tell that the Cult Lord’s final words contained his true feelings.

Perhaps the Cult Lord had wished for his son to speak to his older brother in his stead. Because the son would have been able to explain his own feelings better than he could himself.

He might also have wished for his son to tell him how he should forget his older brother as well. Because if it were him, he would have placed such expectations on Geom Mugeuk.

Hyeolcheondoma slowly walked over to Gyocheon’s side. Gyocheon still stood in place, pierced by the sword. Because pulling out the sword would mean instant death, Geom Ujin did not pull it out, giving Hyeolcheondoma time to bid farewell.

“Master.”

Gyocheon looked at Hyeolcheondoma with eyes losing their vitality.

Hyeolcheondoma’s gaze as he looked at his master was filled with regret and sorrow.

Hyeolcheondoma could guess from the earlier conversation what kind of illusion technique had been deployed. A move that utilized the deep wounds of the past shared by the Cult Lord and himself.

“It must have been fearsome, being the illusion technique Master prepared as his final resort. Yet the reason it failed is likely because neither Master nor I have ever raised a child.”

Gyocheon’s deep wrinkles twitched weakly.

“Master, let us meet again in the next life in a better relationship.”

It was Hyeolcheondoma’s sincere wish. Although he had committed a great wrong this time, he still wanted to meet his master again in the next life.

“In this life, I will forget Master after today. So let us meet in the next life. Let us meet as master and disciple of a small martial hall in some countryside village, with no succession struggles. Then I will truly serve you well. Then let us also have children and raise them well.”

Gyocheon’s pupils trembled. Just as he had felt ever since meeting Hyeolcheondoma this time, he could feel that this disciple truly grieved his death.

Gyocheon left his final words at the end of a coughing fit that expelled blood.

“……Pretense until the very end.”

With those words, Gyocheon breathed his last, eyes wide open.

He did not know what his master’s true feelings were in the final moment, but because Gyocheon had refused to acknowledge him until the very end, Hyeolcheondoma’s heart was rather lightened.

“In the end, it was but a fleeting moment, was it not?”

Hyeolcheondoma closed his master’s eyes with trembling hands.

“Lay down past grudges and please rest in eternal peace.”

Geom Ujin slowly pulled out the sword, and Gyocheon fell backward, spraying blood.

Hyeolcheondoma caught his body and carefully laid him down.

Hyeolcheondoma fulfilled his duty to the very end. He brought firewood, stacked it, and cremated his master.

“Since it would become a grave no one visits, you might as well soar freely into the sky.”

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Ujin stood side by side watching that sight.

Geom Mugeuk knew. Though it had been for his father’s sake, the act of betraying his master had remained a wound in Hyeolcheondoma’s heart for his entire life.

A man who did not use the situation as an excuse but bore that wound willingly in his heart, a man who had lived embracing his own misfortune—this was Hyeolcheondoma.

Through this matter, his father must have realized as well. How deeply that wound had remained. And through this matter, the relationship between those two would change once more.

He could feel that Hyeolcheondoma had sorted out his heart to some extent.

Then what about Father?

Geom Mugeuk did not simply let it pass like this.

One may bury the corpse, but do not bury the wound of the heart.

Because he was one who held such convictions.

“Father, did you see Uncle in that illusion?”

Geom Ujin silently nodded.

“How was it?”

If he had asked this question when he first regressed, his father would never have answered. He probably would have turned away without even replying, especially since it was a question regarding his older brother.

But now it was different. Unexpected words flowed from his father’s mouth.

“It was good to see him after so long.”

In this moment, Geom Mugeuk felt the deep regret his father harbored.

Surely it was because of this very regret that his father wished for a succession struggle in which his children did not shed each other’s blood. Surely his father had missed his older brother too.

“I’m sure Uncle was happy to see you as well.”

Geom Ujin looked at his son.

“He must have wanted to say these words to you. ‘Little brother, you are doing so well. I am relieved that you lead the Cult.’ How do I know? Because he is your older brother? Father’s eldest son. Where would the bloodline of such wonderful sons go? Ah, I should have entered the illusion with you. I had so much I wanted to say to Uncle.”

*Yes, if it had been that boy, he would have talked this much even there.* How could anyone imitate such chatter?

“By the way, then did you return to your youth?”

When Geom Ujin nodded, Geom Mugeuk grew even more regretful.

“Ah! It was the perfect opportunity to see you in your youth. I should have faced off against your younger self with this handsome face of mine. Even if I couldn’t match the Nine Transformations Demonic Art, I should have won at that!”

He had realized this as well. Because he could imagine how much the real son would have talked about his youthful appearance.

Hyeolcheondoma watched that sight and felt envy once again.

Geom Mugeuk did not miss that look.

“You’re jealous, aren’t you? You wish you had a son like this, don’t you?”

How quick-witted he was. No, that wasn’t it. It was because he had inadvertently said to his master earlier that they should have children.

If it had been just the two of them, he would have responded with something like “Not jealous at all; family is the enemy.” But he could not say such things in front of the Cult Lord.

“Do not be jealous. Don’t you have another child, Elder?”

“Another child?”

Geom Mugeuk infused his whistle with internal energy.

Then someone came running toward them from afar like the wind.

The one who arrived in a single breath was Seo Daeryong. He had been hiding and waiting from afar.

“I pay my respects to the Cult Lord.”

Seo Daeryong politely greeted Geom Ujin, then looked at Hyeolcheondoma. Today of all days, he was more nervous to see his master than the Cult Lord.

Seo Daeryong’s heart was fully revealed on his face.

“……Master.”

Even from the trembling voice calling out to Hyeolcheondoma, one could tell how much he had worried and longed to see his master.

“Do you not resent me for dragging you into this?”

Hyeolcheondoma had been entangled in matters with his master and had no choice but to call him, determined to protect him somehow. Fortunately, Geom Mugeuk and the Cult Lord arrived afterward, so he had not worried about Seo Daeryong since.

Seo Daeryong answered Hyeolcheondoma’s question:

“How could this matter have been dragged in by Master? It was because of them. And Master, don’t you find it a shame? Your disciple came and handled everything on his own. Master, it’s all over. Not because I am such a capable disciple, but because I am a timid and fearful one like me.”

Their two gazes intertwined in the air. Geom Mugeuk felt it. That those two completely different people had subtly similar sides.

“Branch Lord Seo, you must not have heard that.”

Because they were before his father, he addressed Seo Daeryong as Branch Lord.

“What do you mean?”

“Elder Doma has declared thus: from now on, I have only one master-disciple relationship.”

In that instant, Seo Daeryong’s face flushed.

In turn, he also conveyed to Hyeolcheondoma what Seo Daeryong had done.

“When we met earlier, Branch Lord Seo said this. That he could give his life for his master. He willingly became dangerous bait.”

Seo Daeryong bowed his head deeply. His face grew even redder. This was why he liked Geom Mugeuk. Because whenever there was something that needed to be conveyed, he invariably conveyed it like this.

His taciturn master might not say much, but having his feelings conveyed was enough.

Then Hyeolcheondoma spoke.

“If something dangerous arises in the future, have the Young Lord do it.”

Seo Daeryong’s head shot up in surprise. Having felt his master’s affection hidden within the jest, Seo Daeryong was overjoyed as if he could take flight.

“You heard that, right? You definitely heard that, right? It’s Master’s official command.”

Geom Mugeuk said as if tattling to his father.

“You heard it too, Father, right? Then you must say something. What kind of words are those to say to my child? From now on, you and your disciple will handle all the dangerous matters!”

A smirk formed on Geom Ujin’s lips. That smirk, which always held many meanings, was speaking thus today:

*You have worked hard too.*

That was why it was the most magnificent smirk to close this day’s occasion.

His father walked out of that place. Hyeolcheondoma followed behind.

Both his father and Hyeolcheondoma appeared to walk with light footsteps.

Geom Mugeuk stood for a moment, watching the backs of the two.

Originally, Hyeolcheondoma had followed one step behind, but his father slowed his pace and moved shoulder to shoulder.

The two spoke as they walked. Perhaps they were speaking of that day’s matter, which they had left unspoken for all these years.

Watching their backs, he suddenly thought of the line Hyeolcheondoma had drawn when he first met him after regressing.

The line he had drawn, saying it was merely because the Cult Lord cherished him. Because these were two people who possessed such a past.

*Elder, that line you drew back then was correct.*

Chapter 521: May I Cast One Last Divination?
--------------------------------------------
As I watched the backs of my father and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, a martial artist from Tongcheon Pavilion came and reported.

“We have located the whereabouts of the Ungang Sect’s people, and by order of the Tongcheon Pavilion Lord, the demon troops have been deployed for a rescue operation.”

This place where we had fought Gyo Cheon was none other than the Ungang Sect. Fortunately, they had not killed them, but kept them alive.

Geom Mugeuk said to the Tongcheon Pavilion martial artist,

“Send word to the Myeolma Squad Leader that we’ll meet him there.”

“Yes! Understood.”

Before leaving, I had to see my friend’s face. In dealing with this matter, I had received a great deal of help from Jin Hagun.

When the Tongcheon Pavilion martial artist disappeared with brisk lightness skill, Seo Daeryong, who had been listening to the report beside me, rejoiced.

“What a relief.”

“You were the one who found these people.”

The last two sects Seo Daeryong had picked were the Bongsan Sword Gate and the Ungang Sect. He had said he simply could not choose between the two, and Geom Mugeuk had chosen the Bongsan Sword Gate. But that side had been a trap to stall for time, and the incident had taken place here at the Ungang Sect.

“As expected! Such is the power of entering Hwangcheon Pavilion at the top of the class!”

He could have puffed out his chest and swaggered at my praise, but Seo Daeryong bowed his head politely and expressed his gratitude.

“Young Cult Leader, thank you.”

“For what?”

“Just… for everything.”

If he had met his master alone, would such a wonderful reunion as today’s have happened? I did not think so. It would have been awkward and distant, and he would not have been able to convey his true feelings at all.

“Write down all that gratitude! Just wait until later. Even that fine head of yours will turn into one that forgets the moment you turn around. ‘Ah, the Young Cult Leader said that to me back then? I feel like I did it myself.’ You’ll make me jump out of my skin with your distorted memories. No, no. I won’t remember either anyway. So write it all down, meticulously, without leaving anything out.”

“I’ll make it into a book and pass it down through the generations.”

In Seo Daeryong’s heart, an image of the future took shape.

The image of himself sitting his grandchildren on his knees and telling them about this day.

Perhaps those grandchildren might say this.

—Grandfather, is it another story about the Cult Leader?

There was no helping it. That gratitude had piled up like a mountain.

Even today’s events were like that. Geom Mugeuk could have told him to wait at the safe house, but he had brought him all the way here.

“I’m grateful that you brought me here so I could meet Master even a little sooner.”

“That’s not why I brought you.”

“What? Then why did you bring me?”

“If possible, you should meet at the place where all grudges and debts are resolved. Meeting him after waiting at the safe house and meeting him here would have felt very different. In a martial artist’s life, what matters most is whether you were there, in that place, at that moment.”

It sounded right. The excitement he had felt when he saw his master in this place drenched with blood was still vivid.

“Even though I couldn’t fight with you?”

As a martial artist, how could he not have such regret?

“You did fight with us. Not stepping forward for no reason to help your master, only to become a hostage and a nuisance—that alone means you fought well with us. I like that you’re smart enough that I don’t have to worry about that.”

Seo Daeryong’s eyes trembled. Just what sin had this Young Cult Leader committed against him in a past life to be like this?

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not one to simply watch Seo Daeryong’s emotion, which showed from his face.

“And if I happen to die, you’ll jump in, won’t you? How could I go on that long road to the underworld without you? I’d be bored.”

He speaks as if he loves taking credit, but in truth, our Young Cult Leader really hates doing so.

Hiding that thought, Seo Daeryong said with an incredulous expression,

“As expected! You had ulterior motives. I’m someone who doesn’t even know how grateful I should be that you told me to hide far away over there. I’m a man whose supreme task is to survive for a long, long time! Fortunately, I fulfilled my dream today. Now I’m not just your right arm. I’m the right arm that doesn’t have to do dangerous work.”

He was referring to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s words that dangerous matters should be left to the Young Cult Leader.

“But what can we do? Since he said to leave the dangerous work to me, you, my right arm, will have to follow the body.”

“Ah, does it turn out that way again?”

Geom Mugeuk strode forward.

“Now then, still-dangerous right arm. Let’s go.”

Seo Daeryong stood there for a moment.

Just as Geom Mugeuk looked at the backs of his father and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, Seo Daeryong silently looked at Geom Mugeuk’s back.

He was a Young Cult Leader who was always joking around and talking a lot, but whenever Seo Daeryong looked at his back, he often thought he looked lonely. The more he knew him, the more it seemed so.

‘If you had died today, Young Cult Leader, my skills are still lacking, so I would not have been able to avenge you. But the road you took would not have been boring. The boatman ferrying us across the road to the underworld would have covered his ears, saying we were too noisy.’

* * *

The sect leader of the Ungang Sect looked up at the sky and gave thanks.

‘Thank you. Truly, thank you.’

Until just a short while ago, he had been devastated.

Experts had broken into the Ungang Sect, subdued him, his blood kin, and his disciples, and locked them all in this remote storehouse.

He was a renowned expert in Chongqing, but he had been no match for the experts who attacked.

Among the attackers, there were two he recognized. Two who plunged him into despair.

The red-faced Red-Eyed Young Master.

The man with the image of a woman tattooed on his body was the Lust-Soul Vajra.

Especially when he saw the Lust-Soul Vajra, his heart sank. He knew all too well how many women that bastard had raped and killed.

Not only his wife and daughter—how could there be only one or two women in his clan?

Such vicious men had come in droves.

‘In the end, we’ll all die miserably.’

Just as he was in that state of despair,

the sound of something clashing came from outside. Then came screams.

Kwa-deu-deu-deuk.

The massive iron door was torn away, and someone filled the doorway.

When he saw that face, the sect leader was startled. It was his first time seeing a martial artist so huge, and his first time seeing one with such a frightening face.

The huge martial artist said in a blunt tone,

“We’ve come to rescue you. Everyone, come out.”

He looked even more like a villain than the villains who had captured them.

Yet they had saved him.

“Thank you. Truly, thank you. I will never forget this kindness.”

Even when he asked for the names of his benefactors, the large martial artists only told him to wait a little and did not reveal their identities.

As he was calming his startled heart, Chongqing Branch Leader Ju Gweol arrived with martial artists from the Martial Alliance.

When the Ungang Sect Leader saw a familiar face, he was completely relieved.

After exchanging greetings with the Ungang Sect Leader, Ju Gweol approached Jang Ho.

“We have received your help again.”

The look in his eyes as he gazed at Jang Ho had changed not merely because he had received help.

By now, Ju Gweol knew that the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was not behind this matter.

He had been told that the Azure Cloud Divine Hero had been practicing the Myeolcheon Demon Blade Form at the Bongsan Sword Gate, and that he had planned this entire affair. Thus, his misunderstanding toward the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had been cleared.

“I’m sorry I misunderstood you.”

And earlier, when the branch had been attacked, he had heard from Jin Hagun that these large martial artists who had saved him were demon troops of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

He truly disliked and hated the Demonic Cult, but the gaze he turned toward Jang Ho was gentle. Not only had they saved his life and the lives of his subordinates, they had also prevented the annihilation of the Ungang Sect.

“I’m merely someone who follows orders.”

Because Jang Ho did not put on airs at all, Ju Gweol liked him all the more. Perhaps it was because he felt sorry for having misunderstood them. Surprisingly, he thought it might not be bad to form a friendship with such a person.

Was friendship possible between a demonic practitioner and a martial artist of the orthodox sects?

He had been truly displeased by Jin Hagun’s friendship with the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, and yet, as if punishment were being handed down for that displeasure, he found himself feeling this way.

“I hope there will come a day when I can repay you.”

Ju Gweol spoke sincerely, and Jang Ho nodded with a blunt expression.

Naturally, Ju Gweol’s gaze turned toward the two people standing on the roof of a building in the distance.

There, Geom Mugeuk and Jin Hagun stood side by side, talking.

Geom Mugeuk told him honestly why this matter had occurred. He told him everything as it was.

“So that’s what happened.”

When Jin Hagun learned that this incident had arisen because of an old grudge, he once again realized just how frightening a person’s resentment could be.

“On behalf of our cult, I sincerely apologize for the clan that was annihilated because of this incident.”

“It was something you couldn’t help. Even so, thanks to you, they were able to survive.”

If not for Geom Mugeuk, the Ungang Sect would have been destroyed. And if the conflict between both sides had deepened because of this matter, who knew what might have happened? Thus, it was not only the Ungang Sect that had been saved.

“As expected, you did it.”

Jin Hagun looked at Geom Mugeuk. To be honest, while waiting for news at the safe house, he had believed that Geom Mugeuk would resolve it.

“It was thanks to your consideration. If you had put principles first and acted like someone rigid and inflexible, this matter would have been several times more difficult.”

If it had been his past self, he certainly would have put principles first. Had he done so, he might have danced exactly to the tune of those who had plotted this affair.

“If I’m to spend time with you, being rigid won’t be easy.”

“Of course. I’ll break through everything one way or another.”

Jin Hagun could feel it. Each time their meetings continued, his relationship with Geom Mugeuk deepened little by little. Because he trusted him, he had allowed demonic practitioners into the Chongqing Branch and had obediently gone into the safe house.

“Is it all over now?”

“The Blade Demon is taking care of the last matter. Once that is finished, we’ll leave immediately.”

Their gazes intertwined in midair.

“Soon, let’s gather the rising talents and see one another.”

Geom Mugeuk lightly launched himself away, vanishing from sight in an instant.

Because there was the expectation that they would meet again soon, the parting did not feel regretful.

Jin Hagun looked at the sky where Geom Mugeuk had flown, then looked below.

His eyes met Ju Gweol’s as the latter turned back after sending off the demon troops.

Every time Ju Gweol had seen him with Geom Mugeuk, his face had twisted into a frown, but today his expression was unusually gentle.

‘Whatever the reason, change like that.’

Because he felt that the power of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was growing greater and greater, just like the massive physiques of the demon troops.

‘If we don’t want to lose, we too must keep changing.’

* * *

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon sat down before the old fortune-teller.

The old man’s expression darkened. The news he had been waiting for had not come, and the person who should not have come had arrived.

“The place you told me was wrong.”

The old fortune-teller had told the Blood Heaven Blade Demon to go to the Bongsan Sword Gate. But Gyo Cheon had been carrying out his scheme at the Ungang Sect.

“Did you perhaps meet my master?”

At the old fortune-teller’s question, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon nodded.

The fact that he had met his master and yet appeared safely before him meant that their plan had failed.

“He untied the knot.”

The one who should have untied that knot had been Gyo Cheon.

No matter how strong the Heavenly Demon was, he had believed that he would not be able to face the twelve of them and then avoid even Gyo Cheon’s final move.

The old fortune-teller sighed.

“I misread the divination.”

When he had met the Blood Heaven Blade Demon previously, he had read his own fortune.

The divination he drew then was: those who will live shall live, and those who will die shall die. He had thought he was on the side that would live.

When the old man sensed his end, his heart instead grew peaceful.

“While planning this affair, I cast countless fortunes, but one thing kept nagging at me. Perhaps, in the larger sense, this matter failed because of that.”

“What is it?”

After a brief pause, the old man brought up something unexpected.

“Your Young Cult Leader.”

The old fortune-teller let out a sigh.

“I simply could not grasp his divination. At times, it came out as though he held the fate of the martial world in his hands; at other times, it came out as though he had no influence on the martial world at all. Sometimes he became a great baleful star that would bring about bloodshed, and sometimes the divination of a great grandmaster who would save the martial world appeared. Each time I cast his fortune, his future was far too different. In all my years of divination, this was the first such case.”

He made a request of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“May I cast one last fortune about the Young Cult Leader’s future?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon nodded.

The old fortune-teller shook the divination tube filled with bamboo sticks. Knowing it would be his last, the old man’s hand movements were careful.

And so one divination stick sprang out.

When the two men saw the characters written on the bamboo, both were startled.

There were four characters written there.

—Unification Under Heaven.

After staring blankly for a moment, the old fortune-teller laughed loudly.

“If so, then my death is the will of heaven.”

The old fortune-teller tilted the divination tube in his hand toward the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Pwaaaaaang!

Hidden weapons shot forth from the tube. It had been both a divination tube and, at the same time, a concealed weapon. At this distance, it was the old man’s unique hidden weapon that no one could avoid.

Chang-chang-chang-chang!

But the hidden weapons were blocked by something and all bounced away. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon had raised the Myeolcheon Great Blade and blocked them.

Shiiik!

The great blade flew in and carved deeply through the old fortune-teller’s chest.

Bone and flesh split apart, and the old fortune-teller collapsed. If he had truly meant to ambush him, it would have been far more difficult to block than this; this attack had been one asking to be killed.

“Since I failed because of one who has received the mandate of heaven… I have no regrets.”

After leaving those final words, the old fortune-teller breathed his last.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon picked up the bamboo stick that had fallen to the floor. He silently looked down at it, then tried to burn it away with his Blazing Yang True Qi.

But soon he withdrew his true qi and, for some reason, placed it inside his robe.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk arrived there.

“Ah, is it over? I ran over hoping to have my fortune told at least once.”

“What fortune would you have read by a charlatan?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon strode away.

“Senior, let’s go together!”

Geom Mugeuk followed after him. Had something happened with the old fortune-teller? Geom Mugeuk sensed something different than usual from the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Oh, and don’t think of going back to the main cult alone. When we return, we all go together.”

He said it out of concern that the man might find his father uncomfortable and simply leave on his own. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was more than capable of doing so.

After a brief pause, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon answered.

“I intended to go together.”

It seemed that, while speaking with his father, he had decided then to go with them.

“Ah, good. There will be even more enjoyable things on the way back.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon and Seo Daeryong would be joining him on the journey back to the main cult. Just as he had spent much time with his father, he hoped the two of them would have such an opportunity as well.

“Ah, and look forward to it. There’s a real contest left.”

“What contest?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked at Geom Mugeuk with a curious expression.

But Geom Mugeuk did not tell him, as if to say he would find out later. Instead, he smiled meaningfully and said,

“You’ve never tried the fish dish my father makes, have you?”

Chapter 522: You Have to Win a Wager.
-------------------------------------
Early in the morning, Hwi checked the horses, inspected the carriage, and loaded the luggage. At last, today was the day they would return to the Main Sect.

They decided to have the Blood Heaven Doma and Seo Daeryong ride in the carriage they had come in. It was a large carriage with plenty of room, but Seo Daeryong was the problem.

“I can ride up on the coachman’s seat, right?”

“You’re the Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion. You should ride inside with us.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s answer, Seo Daeryong flinched. Travel that long road together with the Cult Leader and his master?

“I’ll age ten years on the way. I won’t be able to breathe properly or sleep for even a moment. Please, let me ride on the coachman’s seat. Please!”

He could tease the man if he made him ride inside with them, but Seo Daeryong was so desperate that Geom Mugeuk relented.

“Actually, I’m not the one you should be asking for permission.”

Geom Mugeuk brought Seo Daeryong to Hwi.

“You’re the only one who can save our Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion.”

Though he said no more than that, Hwi understood what he was asking.

Hwi said to Seo Daeryong,

“The coachman’s seat will be more uncomfortable than you think.”

“That’s all right. I’ll take turns driving, so you can rest comfortably when I do.”

A carriage driven by Seo Daeryong? Geom Mugeuk couldn’t let that pass.

“What if the carriage jolts while you’re driving? What if my father happens to be drinking tea at that exact moment and splashes it all over his face? If it jolts, your neck will be severed! Can you handle that?”

Even though he knew it was a joke, Seo Daeryong grew serious.

Hwi reassured him.

“No matter how much the carriage jolts, the tea will never spill onto the Cult Leader’s face.”

The Cult Leader was the sort of man who could have a whole jar of venom poured over him and still walk away without getting a single drop on him.

Besides, Seo Daeryong would never actually have to drive. The Cult Leader would never entrust the reins of his carriage to someone else.

“All right. I’ll go with you.”

Once Hwi gave his permission, Seo Daeryong thanked him with the look of a man who had narrowly escaped death.

“Thank you so much.”

Meanwhile, Geom Ujin and the Blood Heaven Doma came outside.

Geom Ujin climbed into the carriage first, followed by the Blood Heaven Doma. The two sat facing each other, while Geom Mugeuk took the seat beside the Blood Heaven Doma.

Hwi’s voice came from the coachman’s seat.

“Then we’ll be setting off.”

The carriage left the villa and began to roll along.

His father closed his eyes and practiced qi circulation, while the Blood Heaven Doma read a book.

After watching the two of them for a moment, Geom Mugeuk leaned out through the window on the opposite side. The wind whipped his robes.

“Uncle, you know we’re taking the long way back, right?”

* * *

When the carriage stopped beside a river, Geom Mugeuk told the Blood Heaven Doma what he had been saving for him.

“That contest is a fishing contest.”

“Fishing?”

When the Cult Leader had asked him whether he had ever tasted the fish dishes the young master cooked, he had suspected something, but he never imagined it would really be a fishing contest.

“The loser has to cook. This is the third contest.”

“Who won the previous ones?”

“I’m very curious about Father’s third fish dish.”

The Blood Heaven Doma looked at Geom Ujin. His eyes seemed to say, “No, did you really lose to your son twice?”

Geom Ujin looked from his triumphant son to the Blood Heaven Doma, whose gaze seemed to ask how he could possibly have lost, then silently walked toward the carriage.

He brought out the fishing rods from the carriage. They were the very ones he had spent all that time making at their lodgings.

“It’s a fishing rod imbued with Father’s devotion. It’s worth more than the East Sea Fisherman’s personal weapon.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Blood Heaven Doma subtly provoked his father.

“There are many things more important than equipment when it comes to fishing.”

Sure enough, one corner of his father’s mouth lifted slightly.

“Have you fished before, Elder?”

The Blood Heaven Doma shrugged.

“I did my share of fishing in my younger days.”

This was not a provocation Geom Mugeuk could simply ignore.

“How about it? Would you like to join the contest as well? It doesn’t matter to me either way, since I’ll be coming in first.”

Could his father possibly stand by while his pride was challenged?

“I don’t mind.”

Geom Mugeuk even brought Seo Daeryong into the contest.

“Since we’re doing this, let’s make it four people. Father and son, master and disciple—who will come in last?”

They had made several fishing rods while competing against his father, so there were more than enough.

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Ujin picked up their rods first and walked toward the river.

The scenery was breathtakingly beautiful. Nameless wildflowers bloomed along the riverbank, and the river flowed quietly, its surface glittering with fragments of sunlight.

The mere fact that he was fishing with his father in a place like this put Geom Mugeuk in an exceptionally good mood.

“Have you prepared a new recipe?”

The contest began with Geom Mugeuk’s provocation.

Whoosh.

His father’s hand was incomparably smooth and supple as he cast his line.

The line flew straight over the water before sinking beneath its surface.

Truly, even the way his father cast a fishing line looked as though he were executing a supreme martial art.

But fishing isn’t martial arts, Father. When I think of all the fish I’ve caught in my life, I’m sorry to say it, but you still have a long way to go.

Geom Mugeuk began fishing in earnest as well.

Watching the two of them fish, the Blood Heaven Doma felt his own blood begin to boil. When it came to competitiveness, he was second to no one.

‘Cult Leader, of all things, you’re losing at fishing to a child? I’ll show you my skill.’

Overflowing with confidence when it came to fishing, the Blood Heaven Doma walked toward the river with Seo Daeryong.

“There are places where fish bite well.”

“What about the spots where the Cult Leader and Young Cult Leader are standing?”

The Blood Heaven Doma shook his head.

“They may know how to kill people, but what do those two know about fishing? Let me see.”

With the eyes of an expert, the Blood Heaven Doma examined the riverbank.

“There. Where the current is slow, with stones, rocks, or aquatic plants, and shade beneath the trees. Fish gather in places like that.”

He took up a position beneath the drooping branches of a large willow tree.

He also found a spot for his disciple.

“You fish over there. It’s just as good as my spot.”

“Thank you, Master!”

Seo Daeryong’s spot was between the Blood Heaven Doma and Geom Mugeuk.

As he cast his line with an awkward motion, Seo Daeryong said,

“This is my first time fishing in my life. What I do know is that the true charm of fishing is waiting! It’s the patience to fish for the passing years!”

“Who told you that? The charm of fishing is the feel of a fish on the line! That one moment makes it worth waiting all day! Understand?”

“Yes, Master!”

And so, the four of them began fishing beside the gently flowing river.

Hwi stood in front of the carriage, parked some distance away, and watched them.

They had experienced countless unexpected things on this journey, and now another had been added to the list.

Who would have imagined that he would get to see the Cult Leader, the Young Cult Leader, the Blood Heaven Doma, and his disciple fishing together?

The fishing rod of the man who had created this astonishing change bent sharply.

“I’ve got one! First bite!”

With practiced skill, Geom Mugeuk reeled it in without letting it escape.

“It’s big! It’s huge!”

Grinning brightly, Geom Mugeuk held up the fish he had caught and proudly showed it to everyone.

A flash of disappointment crossed Geom Ujin’s face at having missed the first fish.

Only Hwi saw that fleeting expression. Had he ever seen such disappointment on the Cult Leader’s face before? No matter how hard he thought, he couldn’t remember. That brief expression was therefore immensely precious to Hwi.

How much more time passed?

The next person to haul in a fish was, unexpectedly, Seo Daeryong.

“Oh! I caught one!”

Seo Daeryong really had hooked a fish.

“It’s my first! This is the first fish I’ve ever caught in my life!”

Seo Daeryong jumped up and down in delight.

The Blood Heaven Doma came over and showed him how to remove the fish from the hook. As he placed it in the net, he spoke to the fish.

“To be caught by such a novice, you must be a remarkably scatterbrained creature yourself.”

And indeed, this river was home to many blind and scatterbrained fish.

“I caught another one!”

Seo Daeryong shouted joyfully.

“Beginners are usually lucky. That’s how it is at first.”

The Blood Heaven Doma’s gaze, now slightly anxious, turned toward Geom Ujin. At the sight of the determination in the Cult Leader’s eyes—the absolute refusal to lose—the Blood Heaven Doma’s own fighting spirit blazed up.

Then a cry extinguished that flame.

“I caught another one!”

This time, Geom Mugeuk hauled in a fish.

“Oh! Young Cult Leader! That’s a big one!”

Seo Daeryong, who had been celebrating with him, suddenly shuddered.

“Why does it suddenly feel so cold?”

Geom Mugeuk laughed at his words. A chill was flowing from his father on the left and from the Blood Heaven Doma on the right.

Only after belatedly sensing the aura did Seo Daeryong realize his mistake. He had been so excited by his first fishing catch that he had failed to read the atmosphere. He reproached himself.

‘You idiot, Daeryong! You’ve survived your whole life by reading the mood, and what are you doing now? Don’t get caught anymore. Don’t catch anything!’

But something had gone wrong. Every time Geom Mugeuk caught a fish, Seo Daeryong caught one too.

Yet he couldn’t deliberately let them go. If he did, his master would surely thunder at him.

Geom Mugeuk reeled one in, then Seo Daeryong reeled one in. As if it were a lie, the fish would bite only the younger men’s bait.

Then, for the first time, Geom Ujin hauled in a fish.

“Congratulations, Father. It’s the biggest fish you’ve caught so far.”

The tension melted from Geom Ujin’s face, while the Blood Heaven Doma grew anxious.

After half an hour had passed, the scores were as follows:

Geom Mugeuk: eight fish. Seo Daeryong: six. Geom Ujin: three. Blood Heaven Doma: three.

They had decided to compete for one hour, so half the time remained.

Geom Ujin reeled in his line and walked somewhere. He headed straight for the space between Seo Daeryong and the Blood Heaven Doma. He had moved spots because he sensed that he would lose if things continued this way.

When the Cult Leader came to stand beside him, Seo Daeryong slowly and stealthily began inching toward Geom Mugeuk.

―Young Cult Leader, please move farther to the side!

―You have a talent for assassination!

Seo Daeryong was gradually moving away from Geom Ujin, bit by bit, without making it obvious.

Even then, Seo Daeryong’s float sank again.

‘No!’

He pleaded with the heavens not to let him catch another fish, but the fish adored Seo Daeryong too much.

And so Seo Daeryong moved toward Geom Mugeuk, while Geom Ujin and the Blood Heaven Doma stood side by side and fished.

The Blood Heaven Doma saw the fiery determination in Geom Ujin’s eyes—the heat of a man who refused to lose. He had not come over to speak with him. He had truly come to catch fish.

‘Yes. This was the kind of competitive man the Cult Leader was.’

Then he suddenly remembered the fortune slip tucked against his chest.

‘The Young Cult Leader’s fortune was “Unification of All Under Heaven.”’

How would the Cult Leader react if he saw it?

The Cult Leader’s dream was the unification of the martial world. What if his son’s fortune was the unification of all under heaven?

He could not tell what fate awaited the two of them.

Beyond the river, clouds drifted slowly between the ridges of the mountains. Geom Ujin, who had been watching those clouds, abruptly spoke to the Blood Heaven Doma.

“It’s been a long time since I last went fishing.”

The Blood Heaven Doma was startled by those words.

“You remember?”

Geom Ujin nodded.

They had gone fishing together in their youth. It had happened only once. At the time, the Blood Heaven Doma had taught Geom Ujin how to fish—what bait was best, where fish gathered, and everything else, just as he had taught Seo Daeryong. It had been Geom Ujin’s first time fishing.

“I must have taught you poorly.”

At Geom Ujin’s words, the Blood Heaven Doma gave a faint smile.

That day remained as vivid as if it had happened only yesterday.

After passing through several decades, they were once again standing side by side and fishing.

“We have to win the wager. I’ll find a new spot.”

The Blood Heaven Doma’s eyes gleamed as he began looking around.

* * *

Half an hour later.

Geom Ujin was cleaning the fish.

Beside him, the Blood Heaven Doma was preparing the vegetables.

The last place went to Geom Ujin, while the Blood Heaven Doma came in third.

Geom Mugeuk, Seo Daeryong, and Hwi watched them.

“At last, I’ve found something I can beat Father at!”

Unlike the excited Geom Mugeuk, Seo Daeryong looked as though the heavens had collapsed.

He had caught the second-most fish. He had defeated, all at once, the two people in the world he absolutely must never defeat.

“I was lucky because Master found such a good spot for me.”

Of course, those words did nothing to comfort the Blood Heaven Doma, who had come in third.

“Luck is also a skill.”

At the Blood Heaven Doma’s words, Seo Daeryong sent Geom Mugeuk a transmission with a half-dazed expression.

―Surely they won’t hold this against me over something like this, will they?

―If you knew how much effort Father put into making that fishing rod, you couldn’t say something like that.

―Then what’s going to happen to me now?

―What’s going to happen? He’ll step down as the Master of the Hwangcheon Pavilion and become an investigator again. The elder will choose a new personal disciple. I hear there’s a truly kind one among his existing disciples. You’re fine, aren’t you? You don’t have any ambition for success or fame, do you?

―……I have plenty of both.

Even as they spoke, Geom Ujin’s cooking continued.

The Blood Heaven Butcher tossed the vegetables into the air, then swung the Heaven-Slaying Great Saber.

Shk-shk-shk-shk-shk-shk-shk!

The vegetables were sliced thin by the huge saber and fell into the bowl. It was a wondrous sight unlike anything they had ever seen.

Father played along with the Blood Heaven Butcher’s excitement. When he extended his hand, the jars of seasoning flew from his leather pouch through telekinesis and danced in midair. Red pepper powder scattered like blood, while salt fluttered down like snow.

Both of them looked delighted. Father, in particular, seemed to be in an even better mood than during the previous three rounds of cooking, though he had every reason to feel displeased.

So Geom Mugeuk asked Hwi in a roundabout way.

“It’s different from last time, isn’t it?”

“It does seem that way.”

There was no way Father, the very incarnation of competition, could be this pleased after losing three matches in a row.

Geom Mugeuk found one possible reason.

“Could it be because he wanted to cook for the elder? Or is he simply enjoying this moment of cooking together?”

If not for an opportunity like this, Father would have no chance to cook for the Butcher himself, nor to cook together with him. He never had before, and he never would again.

Hwi smiled and nodded.

‘Do you know this, Young Cult Master? It has been a very long time since the Cult Leader looked this happy.’

Geom Ujin personally placed the fish he had cooked into a bowl and handed it to Seo Daeryong.

“Oh my! You’re giving mine to me first!”

Seo Daeryong accepted the bowl, unsure of what to do.

Geom Mugeuk teased him.

“Second place has every right to eat!”

Seo Daeryong’s face turned deathly pale as he shouted with his eyes. Please, stop! Please stop calling me second place!

The Blood Heaven Butcher played along with Geom Mugeuk’s prank.

“Then third place has no right to eat.”

When the Butcher started to rise from his seat, Seo Daeryong desperately tried to stop him.

“Master! Please, eat your meal!”

Pretending to have been persuaded, the Blood Heaven Butcher sat back down and sent Geom Mugeuk a mental transmission.

―You brat, you should have let your father win.

―I have to win if I want to do this again. You know what Father’s like. He’ll practice fishing and come back.

―Do you really like your father that much?

What should he say?

―Yes, I do.

That one answer would be enough.

The Blood Heaven Butcher looked toward the Cult Leader.

‘How could anyone not be envious of this?’

The bowls filled with food were placed before everyone.

Seo Daeryong was already worried whether he could even swallow such an honored fish dish.

But that concern proved unfounded. The fish dish the Cult Leader had prepared was incredibly delicious.

“It’s delicious! I’m not exaggerating or flattering you—it really is delicious!”

The Blood Heaven Butcher also tasted Geom Ujin’s cooking. Come to think of it, this was the first time he had ever eaten a dish prepared by the Cult Leader.

The Blood Heaven Butcher silently continued moving his chopsticks. Rather than praise the taste, he expressed his approval by completely emptying his bowl.

“This is the most delicious one yet.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly as he looked at his father.

Geom Ujin wore a satisfied expression as he watched his son, the Blood Heaven Butcher, Hwi, and Seo Daeryong eat with relish.

And so the five of them sat around the table and shared a meal. To a passerby, they would have looked like ordinary travelers enjoying a brief meal together.

This was precisely the everyday life Geom Mugeuk so desperately longed for.

Geom Mugeuk turned the scene into a single painting and engraved it in his heart.

‘I prefer this moment to the moments when Father fought and defeated his enemies. That is why I will keep trying to create moments like this.’

After the meal, Geom Mugeuk looked at Seo Daeryong.

Seeing the mischief in the gaze directed at him, Seo Daeryong gave a silent shake of his head. Whatever it is, please don’t!

“Since last place and third place cooked, first place and second place should clean up.”

Chapter 523: The Most Cultured Demon Venerable.
-----------------------------------------------
After finishing their meal, he walked along the riverbank with his father.

He had tagged along when his father went out for a walk alone.

The two stopped for a moment and watched a small boat passing in the distance. Reflected in the glow of the sunset, there was no distinction between the river and the sky. The sunset and sky were split together by the oar that the fisherman standing on the boat rowed.

“I have a few things I would like to ask Father regarding martial arts.”

“What is it?”

“I’m referring to when you fought the twelve experts last time.”

That day’s battle was etched intact in Geommugeuk’s mind. Simply watching that fight had brought him great enlightenment and learning.

And if he could resolve a few things he had wondered about from that fight, it would be of greater help than training alone for several years.

Of course, he was certain it would also serve as a great stimulus to his father. To make the martial arts of an absolute master like his father advance, he had to ask him endlessly. Is this right? Is that right?

Because the learning gained while teaching would have the greatest effect in moving his father’s martial studies.

“When you fought Giryeon Yosa, you unfolded your footwork like this.”

Geommugeuk unfolded the footwork his father had used that day right there on the spot.

When he accurately reproduced that day’s footwork, Geomujin made a surprised expression.

“You memorized all of this?”

“Not just this. I memorized everything from beginning to end.”

Of course, he added a jesting remark as well.

“I have surpassed the limits of my memory, so I must hurry to ask you and empty it out.”

Geommugeuk threw the questions he had prepared one by one.

“At this moment, why did you dodge to the left rather than the right?”

“It was because Heukje, another enemy, seemed likely to attack from the left.”

“Then shouldn’t you have dodged even further to the right?”

“If he sent forth sword qi, I intended to deflect that sword qi toward Giryeon Yosa and seize the opportunity.”

Geommugeuk recalled his father’s appearance deflecting the incoming sword qi toward Giryeon Yosa and charging at her.

“That certainly seems like it would have been more efficient.”

Because at that time, his father was fighting to achieve maximum effect with minimum internal energy. Moreover, he had been trying to impart a lesson to his son as well.

“Then if someone else had broken in at that time, how should one respond? With the remaining ones putting two here, and there as well…”

Thus, conversation begetting conversation, the two’s dialogue created new battles, and the imagined battles of the two absolute experts became a gateway entering once again into the lofty world of martial studies.

When speaking, Geommugeuk did not miss his father’s gestures, gaze, and reactions. Those reactions were just as important as the content.

His father did not speak of things he was not certain about as if he knew them. When he didn’t know something, he slightly shook his head.

He did not pretend to know what he didn’t know just because he was before his son. Only a truly strong person can say they don’t know what they don’t know. Yes, when we are inexperienced, we insist stubbornly. His father was personally showing him what the self-esteem of true experts was like.

There were those looking down at the two from the riverbank.

They were none other than Hyeolcheondoma and Seodaeryong.

The one who spoke first was Seodaeryong.

“The Young Cult Leader worries that a war between the demonic and orthodox sects will break out, yet the Cult Leader and Young Cult Leader themselves seem to keep growing stronger.”

Seodaeryong conveyed his honest thoughts to his master. Because he felt he had grown one step closer to his master this time.

“The Young Cult Leader must know well that if one becomes so strong that the balance of power collapses, the possibility of war breaking out also increases. Why would he strive so hard to become stronger?”

Hyeolcheondoma said abruptly.

“He might be harboring a dream of unifying the martial world.”

Thinking his master was joking, Seodaeryong laughed.

“How could that be?”

“How can you know what lies inside a person? Moreover, how can you know what lies inside an extraordinary person like him?”

But Seodaeryong spoke as if certain.

“I may not know the Young Cult Leader’s inner thoughts, but I am certain of this one thing. There is absolutely no war in that unfathomable heart of his.”

Hyeolcheondoma turned his head and looked at Seodaeryong. Though his master was fearsome and difficult, in this moment alone, he was dignified.

“There are many things I don’t know, but I can be certain of that. Had he been such a person, he would never have kept someone like me as his right-hand man.”

The words *“So what about you?”* almost left Hyeolcheondoma’s mouth.

Yes, how could his own thoughts be any different from Seodaeryong’s? It was just that he knew well that when the bastard called fate pushes, humans are helplessly pushed back.

After watching the two in the distance discuss martial studies for a moment, he asked Seodaeryong.

“Have you been training well?”

Seodaeryong was flustered by the sudden question.

“Show me your skills.”

Seodaeryong hadn’t expected his master to ask him to demonstrate martial arts here. He had wondered if he was going to be scolded for being stubborn for no reason, but that wasn’t it.

“They are racing ahead so fearlessly; we must work ourselves to death just to follow even halfway.”

Hyeolcheondoma corrected his disciple’s posture and began to transmit anew the essence of the Heavenly Annihilation Demon Blade Art.

By the river were Geommugeuk and Geomujin; on the riverbank were Hyeolcheondoma and Seodaeryong.

And Hwi, leaning against the carriage, was looking up at the sunset-stained sky.

Their training continued until the sunset became dawn.

* * *

The journey continued.

The return journey was far more free and leisurely than the journey out.

Is it really okay to be so leisurely while escorting the Demon Cult Leader back? Hwi had thought this many times.

But no one hurried.

They all surely knew. That this journey was not one that would come again easily.

Geommugeuk especially got involved in all sorts of things. If another carriage was stuck in a rut along the way, he naturally got down to help, and if there was a family with children carrying heavy loads, he gave them a ride in the carriage.

At scenic spots, he had the carriage stop and everyone got out to sightsee.

Today, something caught Geommugeuk’s eye again.

“Uncle Hwi, please wait a moment.”

As the carriage stopped, Geommugeuk got down and approached an old couple working in the field.

“We’ll help you with the work, so would you make us a bowl of noodles as a midday snack?”

Having someone work for a bowl of noodles would normally be a grateful and welcome thing, but the other party was martial artists wearing swords.

When the old couple made guarded expressions, Geommugeuk spoke to them with a smile.

“We’re not bad people, so don’t worry. We’re just feeling a bit peckish while traveling.”

“If you wish, we will simply treat you to noodles.”

This was how common people normally treated martial artists.

“No, we couldn’t do that. We will help you with the work.”

The old couple wondered what was going on, but they felt courtesy and dignity in Geommugeuk’s attitude toward them.

“Then please do so.”

“Thank you.”

As Geommugeuk went out to work, Seodaeryong followed.

The old couple explained to the two what work they should help with.

The two began working as instructed.

He could have done it in an instant using martial arts, but Geommugeuk did not. He simply worked up a sweat like an ordinary person.

Geomujin and Hyeolcheondoma watched the scene from the carriage.

“It seems the Young Cult Leader wants to create memories of farming with the Cult Leader.”

He simply could not bring himself to ask his father to farm.

“Can you endure five years against such a son?”

To the words spoken like a joke, Geomujin’s reaction was unexpected.

Geomujin silently watched his son working and then asked Hyeolcheondoma.

“Why don’t you ask him?”

“What do you mean?”

“Whether he can endure me?”

Geomujin got down from the carriage and rolled up his sleeves.

And as he walked toward Geommugeuk and Seodaeryong, he said.

“Working so clumsily, when do you plan to finish?”

Only then did Hyeolcheondoma understand the meaning of the Cult Leader’s words.

It meant that as they grew closer, it would become difficult for Geommugeuk to refuse his intentions as well.

With a father who had indulged his son’s wishes to this extent, could the son really go against that intention?

*The Cult Leader has no intention of losing.*

Hyeolcheondoma was curious about that day. Who would have their will broken that day, five years from now?

Hyeolcheondoma sent a telepathic message to Seodaeryong.

*—Daeryong, quietly withdraw.*

Like the movement of a master angler’s line, Seodaeryong inconspicuously left the spot.

Geommugeuk and Geomujin took up hoes and began farming in earnest.

The other three watched the scene with reverent hearts.

For a Heavenly Demon to be farming was a sight they would never see again in their lifetimes.

It was likely the first time since the founding of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

They shouldn’t just stand by while the Cult Leader worked, but how could the three not know?

Geommugeuk’s reason for stepping forward might have included consideration for the old couple, but more than that, he wanted to have the memory of farming with his father.

In the past, Hyeolcheondoma might have thought this way.

Can you call it a memory if you force a situation like this? Stop this nonsense!

But now he knew. Memories are like that.

Those transparent acts Geommugeuk had done for him, and for other demonic followers, had all remained as memories.

If one waits for memories to come naturally, the years will slip away.

If someone doesn’t move, there are no memories.

“Daeryong.”

“Yes, Master.”

“You must definitely get married.”

Feeling his master’s regret, Seodaeryong was startled. It was the first time he had heard his master tell him to marry since becoming his disciple.

“Yes, Master!”

He answered energetically, but honestly added.

“I’m not popular with women, so I’m not sure I can do it well.”

Hyeolcheondoma turned his head sharply and shouted.

“What’s wrong with you!”

At those words, Seodaeryong felt his heart grow hot. He was so grateful to his master for saying that.

Meanwhile, Geommugeuk and Geomujin’s farming continued.

Without speaking, the two naturally divided the work.

They pulled weeds, picked out stones, dug the earth, and planted seedlings. The sound of hoes cutting through the earth was pleasant to hear.

“How are you so good even at farming? Truly, the only thing I can beat you at is…”

Swoosh.

A stone Geomujin was sorting grazed past Geommugeuk’s face.

“My hand slipped. There are many stones here.”

Just as a fishing story was about to come out, he had thrown a stone pretending it was a mistake.

“This handsome face nearly got another hole.”

“If you dragged this father out to the fields, you should be prepared for that much.”

Geommugeuk smiled brightly.

“When I meet the successors of the orthodox and unorthodox factions, I’ll start with these words. Hey, you lot. Have you ever plowed a field with your grandfather, with your master?”

Knowing that Geommugeuk was truly someone who would do that, Seodaeryong laughed as he listened. His mouth itched to speak. He wanted to quickly tell everything about what had happened at the pleasure house in front of Jangho and Ian.

Once the two seriously worked, it was truly in the blink of an eye.

Though they didn’t use martial arts, they were no ordinary people.

Shortly after, the old couple brought noodles. They also brought a bottle of liquor. They were astonished to see that the work had already been finished.

“Are you already finished?”

It was work that would have taken the couple several days of hardship.

“I’m starving to death. Please hurry with the midday snack!”

Geommugeuk looked around at the three and said.

“Those who didn’t work don’t get a snack!”

Of course, the old couple had also prepared noodles for the three who had been watching.

“Come here, everyone. Please eat.”

Thus, they all sat around together and ate noodles with the old couple.

At ease, the old couple proudly shared the news that their first grandchild would marry in a few months. They said they had worked here their whole lives raising their children.

And when the carriage carrying Geommugeuk’s party departed, a banknote was placed under Geomujin’s noodle bowl.

It was enough money to hold a grand wedding for the grandchild and have plenty left over. They had been working even harder to add even a penny to their grandchild’s wedding expenses.

The old couple bowed their heads toward where the carriage had left, expressing their gratitude. It felt as if wedding gift money—no, noodle payment—had suddenly fallen from the sky.

The carriage once again sped toward the main compound of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Thus, while Geommugeuk usually created these new experiences, there were times when Hyeolcheondoma stepped up, as he did today.

“Since we’re passing by, could we stop there for a moment?”

The carriage stopped at an old bookstore.

The shop owner recognized Hyeolcheondoma and greeted him. Seeing how comfortably the shop owner treated him, it seemed he only knew him as a martial artist who liked orthodox sect books, and had no idea in his dreams that he was a demonic practitioner.

Everyone scattered through the small bookshop, browsing the books. It was a shop that dealt in ordinary books rather than martial arts manuals or treatises on martial theory.

Geom Ujin looked around the bookstore with his hands clasped behind his back.

“This is your first time in a bookstore like this, isn’t it?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Geom Ujin nodded.

“Thanks to the elder, I learned that the smell of books is a pleasant thing. There probably isn’t a book here he hasn’t read.”

From beyond the bookshelf came the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s reply.

“You only say that because you don’t know how many books there are in the world.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his father and said,

“Father, among all the Demon Venerables throughout history, you have under you the most cultured one.”

His father nodded as if acknowledging it. Geom Mugeuk called out toward the bookshelf on the other side.

“Father says he agrees too!”

“You rascal, you’re too loud in a bookstore!”

As if embarrassed, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon went off somewhere else.

After poking his nose into everything as they browsed the store, Geom Mugeuk also bought a book. It was a book no one had expected.

A Culinary Tour of the Central Plains (中原美食探訪).

“It says a hundred places are introduced here. Whenever you have time, Father, I’ll take you to one place at a time. My new goal is to visit every one of them with you.”

Would he really be able to visit all one hundred famous eateries with his father before he died? Perhaps it was a goal more difficult than unifying the murim.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon bought three or four books, and he also bought one for Seo Daeryong. It was a collection of poems and paintings.

This time, Seo Daeryong had come looking for him in Chongqing after seeing his own poetry-and-painting collection.

That was why he bought him one as a gift, even leaving his signature in it.

The next time you find yourself in danger, send this to me. Then I will be sure to come and save you.

Whether he knew that sentiment or not, Seo Daeryong said loudly,

“I’ll recite these poems impressively in front of women.”

And succeed in getting married! He shouted those words only in his heart.

Geom Mugeuk shook his head and said to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon,

“Now you understand, don’t you? Why your disciple still has no woman.”

At those words, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon flinched.

There was no way the quick-witted Geom Mugeuk would miss that reaction. With an expression that seemed to say, Surely you didn’t recite poems in front of women too, Elder? Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

At the master and disciple’s gazes that seemed to ask, Why on earth not! What’s wrong with that? Geom Mugeuk looked at his father and said,

“Father, before we return to the cult, I think you and I will have to demonstrate how one should seduce a woman.”

The corners of Geom Ujin’s mouth, turned toward the bookshelf, curved into a smile. He had lived his whole life without knowing laughter, yet lately he had been smiling like this from time to time. And the only one who saw that precious smile was Hwi.

Their journey back was not over yet.

Chapter 524: The Heroines Have Sent Food
----------------------------------------
Hwi, who was driving the carriage, looked up at the sky.

Dark clouds were gathering, looking as though they would pour down rain at any moment.

“We will stop by the village today.”

Hwi turned from their original route and headed toward a nearby village.

Geom Mugeuk leaned out the window and said.

“I hope there’s a beautiful young lady in this village who might catch the fancy of our Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord.”

At those words, Seo Daeryong’s face, seated on the coachman’s perch, turned red. Of course, he refused to back down.

“I will recite a poem for that woman!”

Just then, thunder rumbled in the distance.

Rumble!

Seo Daeryong flinched.

‘Even thunder, why now of all times!’

While he was sulking, Hyeolcheon Doma’s voice rang out.

“Looks aren’t everything. A man is about his heart!”

At those words of encouragement to his disciple, Seo Daeryong’s expression brightened radiantly.

“Yes! Master! It is the heart that makes the man!”

If only he had been slightly closer to Hwi, he would have asked him. Swordsman Hwi, which do you prefer? The heart, right? Right?

Feeling his excitement in the wind, Geom Mugeuk, who was leaning out of the carriage, smiled silently. Indeed, once a woman comes to know the kind of person you are, how could she not like you?

The further the carriage entered the main street, the wider the road became.

Next to a fruit merchant haggling with a customer over whether to add one more apple, an old herbal medicine vendor was setting up an awning in preparation for the rain.

A street dumpling vendor waved at Hwi and Seo Daeryong to come and try some, while a fabric shop clerk was taking down the silk displayed outside.

Children ran about amid the bustling sounds and delicious smells.

Geom Woojin watched the scene unfolding outside the window in silence.

If the first purpose of this journey was to make memories with his father, then the second was this very moment. It was his hope that his father would see every corner of the Central Plains with his own eyes.

Father, when war breaks out, we will only focus on where, how, and how many of our cult’s warriors die and kill. Yet in the meantime, that merchant over there will die, and those children too. Those numbers will never appear in our statistics.

That was it. Memories of tilling the fields together were good, but the reason it was precious that Father left a banknote for that old couple’s grandson’s wedding.

That was why every one of these moments was both a leisure outing, and at the same time, a confrontation with his father.

The carriage stopped in front of the largest inn in the village.

Hwi took all six rooms on the second floor and asked the innkeeper to ensure no other guests or servants came up.

After deciding to unpack, rest well, and then eat, everyone went to their respective rooms.

Whenever he had time alone, Geom Mugeuk would invariably enter the space of his Dimensional Shift Technique and train using his Soaring Heaven Secret Art.

But today, he lay flat on his bed.

There were times when, immersed in training, he thought this must be what it meant to be devoured by martial arts. Training on a day like today would have made him feel exactly that. Let’s rest. Today, let’s rest properly.

Lying in bed, he stared blankly at the dark clouds visible through the window, then dozed off in the blink of an eye.

How long had he slept like that?

Sssssshhhh.

He woke to the sound of pouring rain. Waking up to the sound of rain, he thought it felt surprisingly pleasant.

Then he suddenly remembered that before his regression, he had hated rain. Whenever downpours came while he was gathering materials for the great art alone, his loneliness and melancholy had grown even greater.

But now, he liked the rain.

The leisure of rolling around in bed listening to the rain. He envied no one in the world.

But that lasted only a moment. Perhaps because he had lived a life of constant running and rushing, his body grew restless and he could no longer lie still.

He got up from bed and left his room.

In front of Seo Daeryong’s door across the way, he checked for signs of movement inside. He wondered if he might be asleep, but he was awake.

“What are you doing? Can I come in?”

“Yes, please come in.”

Opening the door and entering, he found Seo Daeryong reading a book. It was the poetry collection that Hyeolcheon Doma had bought him.

“Is it interesting?”

“Honestly, it’s not that entertaining… but there are passages that make me fall into contemplation without realizing it.”

Perhaps because he knew Seo Daeryong was an intelligent person who had studied hard? The sight of him reading the poetry collection suited him quite well.

“Since it’s raining, want to have a drink?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Seo Daeryong shut his book as if he had been waiting for just that.

“I’d love to.”

They left the room and passed by the rooms of his father, Hyeolcheon Doma, and Hwi.

When Geom Mugeuk playfully stopped as if to enter one of them, Seo Daeryong whispered from behind, as if he had expected it.

“We can’t!”

A drinking session with Father and Lord Doma? He could clearly see how much the kid would struggle with that.

When they came down to the first floor, over a dozen guests were here and there, eating and drinking.

Geom Mugeuk and Seo Daeryong sat where they could see outside well and ordered drinks and snacks.

Rain was pouring down refreshingly.

Small bamboo trees planted beside the building in front of the inn swayed in the rainwater, and ripples spread endlessly in the puddles.

Travelers wearing rain capes and bamboo hats ran past, and in the distance, people took shelter from the rain under eaves. It was a scene that brought peace to the heart just by watching.

In the meantime, the drinks and food arrived.

“Have a drink, Young Lord.”

Seo Daeryong politely poured him a cup. Geom Mugeuk, having received the drink, filled Seo Daeryong’s cup in return.

“It’s been a while. Drinking just the two of us like this.”

“It is a gathering I have long awaited.”

No matter what anyone said, he was someone who wanted to see Geom Mugeuk, and to be with him.

The two of them emptied their first cups refreshingly.

Then they refilled their cups and wordlessly watched the rain. Listening to its sound, they had no need for snacks.

“The smell of rain is nice.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Seo Daeryong twitched his nose.

“Does rain have a smell?”

“It does.”

“Now that you mention it, I don’t think I’ve ever consciously smelled rain.”

There are things you never see if you don’t pay attention, and scents you can never smell if you aren’t conscious of them.

“It’s good—today’s smell of rain.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Seo Daeryong breathed in through his nose, taking in the scent.

They drank a cup listening to the rain, and drank another reminiscing about old times.

Passing drinks back and forth like that, they drank.

“When did you learn to fish?”

To have defeated the Lord three times—his skill was undeniable. Young Lord, what in the world can’t you do?

“You’re the one who’s good at fishing.”

“That was just luck.”

But as if he had been waiting for just those words, Seo Daeryong’s eyes sparkled. He looked around and lowered his voice.

“I can’t forget that feeling in my hands.”

Clenching and unclenching his hands, pulling and pushing his arms, he mimicked the motions of fishing, unable to hide his excitement.

“Who would have thought I’d beat the Lord and Master at fishing? This is just for you, Young Lord. Don’t tell anyone. Next time we compete, I’m confident in fishing, at least against the Lord and my Master. My Master said it was beginner’s luck, but who knows? Maybe I was born with the talent of a fisherman.”

Geom Mugeuk asked with a smile.

“Compared to that feeling in your hands, how was it when you first executed the Heaven-Destroying Demon Sword Art?”

Seo Daeryong pondered for a moment.

“No matter what, it can’t compare to the Heaven-Destroying Demon Sword Art.”

Just then, Seo Daeryong saw. Geom Mugeuk’s gaze was directed behind his back.

Startled, Seo Daeryong’s voice shrank to the size of ants crawling.

“No, right? Please tell me it isn’t.”

Then, a cold voice came from behind.

“You seemed to be deeply troubled choosing between the feelings in your hands?”

At Hyeolcheon Doma’s voice, Seo Daeryong shot up from his seat.

“Oh no, that’s not it, Master.”

Turning around, it wasn’t just his master. Geom Woojin was standing beside Hyeolcheon Doma. Surely he hadn’t heard the part about thinking he could beat the Lord at fishing?

“If the feeling in your hands is that good, you should put down the Dao and pick up a fishing rod.”

“That’s not it, Master. It’s a misunderstanding.”

Geom Mugeuk stepped in for Seo Daeryong, who had turned pale.

“I saw the two of you coming down and casually threw some bait, and our Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord bit just like that. Fishing for people is more fun than fishing for fish. Come, sit here.”

And so, with Hwi who had been in the back, five people sat together. Geom Mugeuk went around pouring drinks for them.

The charm that rain held for people was the same whether one was the Heavenly Demon, the Young Lord, the Demon Venerable, the Chief Guard, or the Pavilion Lord. Thus, everyone immersed themselves in the atmosphere and drank.

While drinking, Geom Mugeuk looked at his father.

Was it because of the rain? His father’s eyes, which looked unusually lonely today, were not looking at the rain before him. What lay within his pupils was clearly fragments of memories.

‘Who are you thinking about?’

Apart from having grown close to his father, moments like these made him feel that he still did not know his father well.

Instead of asking his father, Geom Mugeuk asked Hyeolcheon Doma.

“Elder, is there someone you think of on rainy days?”

Hyeolcheon Doma shook his head.

“No. What about you?”

“I’m the same. I hate it when it rains because lightness skill gets slippery. I live such a parched life.”

“A young one singing the blues of life!”

Though scolding him, Hyeolcheon Doma was smiling.

“But now I have one.”

“Now you do? What do you mean?”

“The people here. Gathering together today to watch the rain—wouldn’t you think of today whenever it rains?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Hyeolcheon Doma thought of his master.

‘Master, you never met a real hypocrite. That’s why you called me hypocritical.’

Perhaps being called that by his master in the first place was because he had been influenced by this real hypocrite.

‘Master should have tasted this hypocrisy long ago.’

Would his master’s life have changed then? He had said he would stop thinking about his master. But with the rain falling, his master came to mind like this.

Sssssshhhh.

And so, each lost in their own thoughts, they drank while watching the falling rain.

Then, new guests entered the inn.

Those who entered were sword-bearing female warriors. When they took off their rain capes and bamboo hats after finding seats, Seo Daeryong’s eyes widened.

He had been consciously pretending not to look since they entered, but among the three women, there was one who caught his eye.

While he was blankly staring, his eyes met hers.

Inwardly flustered, he saw the woman slightly lower her head in greeting. Seo Daeryong also returned the greeting with a light fist-and-palm salute.

It was merely a common greeting between martial artists, yet Seo Daeryong’s heart pounded fiercely like hammer blows. It was an excitement he could not contain alone.

—Did you see that just now? That woman greeted me. She even smiled.

To Seo Daeryong’s excited sound transmission, Geom Mugeuk replied.

—She smiled at me.

—What are you saying! It was me, definitely me!

Seo Daeryong drank to calm his excitement, but his heart beat even faster.

—Ah, but it’s a shame they seem to be orthodox faction women?

—They’d be relieved on their end.

—Young Lord!

—I’m joking, sorry.

Geom Mugeuk smiled and refilled his cup.

Seo Daeryong drank again and glanced toward the women.

—Ah, our eyes met again. She’s conscious of this side too.

—Which one of the three?

—Don’t look!

Seo Daeryong felt a pleasant excitement he hadn’t felt in a long time. The feeling of being alive, the feeling of ‘I am a man!’

In that moment, a poem flowed from Seo Daeryong’s lips, overcome by his high spirits.

Before Geom Mugeuk could stop him, his face flushed red with drunkenness, Seo Daeryong became a lonely swordsman setting out on a night road in the rain.

“…The lonely shadow hides in the wind, and the dream embraced by the rivers and lakes is answered by the sound of rain.”

Having been exchanging sound transmissions with Geom Mugeuk until now, Seo Daeryong had suddenly recited a poem aloud.

As the eyes of Geom Woojin, Hyeolcheon Doma, and Hwi focused on him, Seo Daeryong realized his mistake.

In that moment, he had seen neither the Lord nor his master. He had thought only of reciting a splendid poem in front of the women.

Could the effect of the poem have worked?

The women glanced this way and exchanged a few words. They were surely talking about the poem he had recited.

Shortly after, the innkeeper brought over a dish.

“The female warriors over there have sent a dish.”

Geom Mugeuk asked.

“Which one of us is it for?”

Seo Daeryong had naturally been expecting this.

—The female warriors said they enjoyed the poem.

Then, the innkeeper set the dish down in one place. Everyone looked at the person in question with surprised expressions. The innkeeper had placed the dish before none other than Geom Woojin.

Geom Woojin was still looking at the falling rain as if he had no interest.

Silence flowed.

Seo Daeryong sent a sound transmission with a pained yet still hopeful voice.

—It probably wasn’t my lady who sent it.

Geom Mugeuk had no leisure to reply to that sound transmission.

He alternated looking between his father and the dish with eyes full of jealousy.

“I think it was brought to the wrong person?”

Geom Mugeuk pulled the dish toward himself.

“At a glance, Father and I look alike, so the innkeeper must have been mistaken. This must be the right place?”

Then Geom Woojin, who until now had shown no reaction while looking outside, turned his head. With an arrogant, unconcealable victor’s smile, he pulled the dish back before himself.

“It’s mine.”

Chapter 525: This Fate Remains Unknown
--------------------------------------
Geom Ujin looked back toward where the women were seated.

He did not smile, nor did he thank them. He merely glanced their way once, but Hwi was inwardly astonished.

Even that alone was truly surprising. The Cult Leader was accepting this cheerful atmosphere.

They would never know whose gaze had just fallen upon them.

When their eyes met Geom Ujin’s, the women greeted him with cupped fists. Seen like this, they resembled one another enough to look like sisters.

They all seemed bright and pleasant in nature, and one could sense they were not the sort to be bound by formality.

And from the expressions with which they looked at his father, Geom Mugeuk admitted defeat. The youngest-looking among them had a face that seemed ready to burst into cheers if he so much as spoke to her.

“The innkeeper wasn’t seeing things after all.”

Following Geom Mugeuk, Seo Daeryong lamented through voice transmission.

—It’s a pitiless jianghu where only handsome men survive.

—I’m handsome too, and I still lost! That makes me feel even worse!

—You wouldn’t say that if you’d recited poetry and lost anyway.

The two men, now comrades in defeat, raised their cups with woeful faces.

Watching their miserable state, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon laughed. He could imagine what sort of voice transmissions they must be exchanging.

Geom Ujin called over the innkeeper and, as a gesture of thanks, ordered a bottle of good wine for the female warriors.

Then he rose from his seat.

“Let us stop here for today.”
