“They are well trained.”

Geom Ujin had grasped the demonic practitioners’ abilities and aura simply by looking at them. Furthermore, their eyes were filled with loyalty toward the cult and its leader.

In addition, Geom Ujin had already learned through a letter what had happened here. From branch management and judgment of the situation to pride in the cult and boldness in dealing with the enemy—Gangdal was a man too valuable to remain in such a small branch.

“Thank you!”

Gangdal had lived diligently until now. He had trained diligently, and he had diligently trained his subordinates as well.

Because he had wanted to escape this small branch, he had worked hard to become someone who overflowed beyond the limits of this small branch.

As a result, he had become a branch leader brimming with confidence, and that confidence became the pride of thinking, How dare some mere Chusal Squad look down on us? Because he had that pride, he had been able to accept the women. In the end, everything had been connected.

Through this incident, Gangdal came to understand that opportunity did not simply drop from the heavens, but ultimately began with oneself.

And Gangdal believed that simply having met the Cult Leader in person had brought sunlight into his life.

At that very moment, a dazzling sun shone brilliantly upon his life.

“I will see you at the main cult.”

Chapter 530: To Ordinary Days.
------------------------------
Gang Dal felt a shudder run through his entire body.

‘See you at the Headquarters.’

It was merely six characters, yet it was more moving than any words he had heard in his entire life.

Even if the wealthiest man under heaven came and said, "I will give you my entire fortune," or if an absolute master came and said, "Become my disciple." He would not trade any other six characters in the world for these six.

Gang Dal felt as if he were dreaming.

‘If this is a dream, never let me wake!’

But it was not a dream. The Lord, whom he had thought he would never see even once in his lifetime, was standing right before him, looking at him. Because a person with such an overwhelming presence could not be conjured even in a dream.

Geom Woojin turned and walked away.

"Thank… you."

It should have been a greeting that rang out loud and clear, yet it came out like a murmur. The moment he spoke, the thought that the word 'thank you' was insufficient made his mind become a jumbled mess.

"Lord!"

A shout burst out of him unintentionally in his fluster at failing to offer a proper greeting.

Geom Woojin slowly turned back toward him.

Gang Dal bowed politely with a flushed face. This was the only thing he could think to say.

"I will see you at the Headquarters."

Gang Dal's voice, which had been dignified even before a demon, was trembling.

Geom Woojin gave a slight nod before turning away again.

The members of the branch gathered to congratulate Gang Dal.

"Congratulations, Branch Chief!"

"Congratulations."

His subordinates were happy, yet also regretful on the other hand. Because Gang Dal had truly been a good leader.

"Now that you are going to the Headquarters, we won't be able to see you."

Parting with his subordinates was regrettable, but advancing to the Headquarters had been his long-cherished wish.

Any position would do. So what if he had to shovel horse dung? It was an order given directly by the Lord.

Dana approached Gang Dal to convey her gratitude.

"Thank you, Branch Chief. Thanks to you, we survived."

Gang Dal shook his head and spoke to her. Since she was a woman whom the Hwacheon Pavilion Lord had personally come to rescue, he now spoke with courtesy.

"I am the one who should thank you. Thank you for coming to find me."

If he had not accepted them, they would have died, and if he had not accepted them, there would have been no opportunity for promotion.

Both of them had striven to uphold their convictions, and obtained the best possible result.

After greeting him and returning, Danyeon spoke to Dana.

"Move aside, I can't see."

When Dana turned around, the figure of Geom Woojin could be seen ahead.

"He is so magnificent."

Now that Danyeon knew he was the Heavenly Demon, he seemed even more magnificent.

"If there were a man like that, I would run to him right away!"

Perhaps because she was excited? What she had whispered only to her sister reached Geom Mugeuk's ears as well.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not about to let it pass.

"Youngest Lady."

"Yes, Young Lord."

No matter how comfortably he approached, the other party was the Young Lord of the Demonic Cult.

"Let us correct your mistaken choice first."

While everyone wondered what he meant, Geom Mugeuk asked with a face full of lingering attachment.

"That day, who was it that thought of sending food to my father? Was it the opinion of all three of you?"

At those words, Seo Daeryong needlessly tensed up and pricked his ears.

"No. It was my idea."

At Danyeon's answer, a bright smile formed on Seo Daeryong's lips. He was happy just knowing that it had not been she who sent the food.

A woman who considered the Lord her ideal type! Ah! Just the thought of it was overwhelming.

Geom Mugeuk brought his face close to Danyeon's.

"You must have seen wrong from afar that day. Come, look at my face clearly up close. Come, our youngest lady. If you were to send food again now, to whom would you send it?"

But Danyeon hesitated to answer. The very act of hesitating was an answer of 'not you.'

Geom Mugeuk made a wounded expression.

"Youngest Lady, that is too cruel."

Geom Mugeuk called out loudly to his father, who was talking with Hyeolcheon Doma in front of the building.

"The youngest lady says she will send food to Father until the end! There is one more person here to take to the Headquarters!"

It was nonsense he had spouted to make his father feel good. He was grateful to his father. For coming all the way here personally to support Seo Daeryong. Because that was also supporting him.

'Since you are my right arm, I shall look after you, shall I not?'

At his son's nonsense, Geom Woojin spoke to Hyeolcheon Doma.

"When will he ever grow up?"

"But you like it, don't you?"

"'Like it' is a stretch. It's chaotic."

Yes, 'chaotic' was exactly the right word when dealing with Geom Mugeuk. But Hyeolcheon Doma knew. Just as he himself did, the Lord was enjoying and relishing this chaos.

If he had not come out with his son, when would he ever experience something like this?

"Shall we take a walk?"

"Let us do that."

The two men walked toward the rear garden.

'My Lord, it is alright to show your feelings now.'

'I cannot stand others boasting about their children, but I shall allow you to boast about yours.'

The two of them walked slowly like that and arrived at the rear garden.

They stood side by side and gazed at the scenery beyond the wall.

The leaves of a giant tree standing beside the wall rustled in the blowing wind, and the birds sitting on the branches took flight in that breeze.

Beyond the birds, white clouds drifted by very slowly, as if they had forgotten time.

"Do you truly intend to win?"

It was a blunt question, but the Lord would have understood. He had once asked the Lord if he could endure five years with such a son. At that time, Geom Woojin had asked in return,

'Why do you not ask him? Whether he can endure me?'

"Would you like me to let him win?"

Hyeolcheon Doma was entirely on the Lord's side. He was prepared to accept whatever the Lord wished, to follow his command.

It had been so before, and after going through the matter with his martial brother together this time, his heart toward the Lord had grown even deeper.

That was why. Because he was truly on the Lord's side. That was why he was saying this. Because he sincerely wished for the Lord's happiness.

"The only person in all the martial world who can afford to let the Young Lord win is you, my Lord."

At the questioning look in his eyes, Hyeolcheon Doma replied calmly.

"Because letting him win is something only the greater person can do."

* * *

"But why did you become targets of the Seven Paths Pursuit Squad?"

Geom Mugeuk asked the women.

Since Dana had formed a connection with Seo Daeryong, he had to be concerned about their safety.

Then Chohui stepped forward and spoke.

"They tried to kill me. The three of you were trying to protect me."

Chohui politely revealed her identity.

"I am a courtesan of Cheonhwaru. I worked at Cheonhwaru in Fenghuang, Hunan Province."

She had already revealed her identity to the three women.

The moment he heard her words, Geom Mugeuk was startled.

Cheonhwaru.

The place owned by Yeojeong, who liked Geukak Soma.

"I must go to the main branch of Cheonhwaru in Guizhou. I must meet the Lord of Cheonhwaru."

"May I ask the reason?"

Chohui answered carefully to Geom Mugeuk's question.

"As one so noble has asked, I ought to give an answer, but this matter is one that I must speak of directly after meeting the Lord of the Tower. I beg for your generous understanding."

Chohui bowed her head apologetically.

Geom Mugeuk did not pressure her.

"Everyone has circumstances they cannot speak of; you have nothing to apologize for."

Geom Mugeuk understood her. Because she would not know of the relationship between the Lord of Cheonhwaru and Geukak Soma. Nor of the relationship between Geukak Soma and himself.

But that did not mean he could reveal such secrets to her here and now.

Then Danyeon stepped forward to reassure her.

"We will escort you all the way to your destination."

Because she was one who took responsibility for any task until the end.

Seo Daeryong looked at her with eyes full of worry. Though he did not know what matter Chohui was being chased for, whoever had orchestrated this was someone with enough money and power to mobilize the Seven Paths Pursuit Squad.

If sent off like this, they would undoubtedly face further danger.

Seo Daeryong looked at Geom Mugeuk, seeking permission with his eyes.

Knowing what his gaze meant, there was no need to exchange voice transmissions.

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

Seo Daeryong spoke carefully to Dana.

"Young Lady Dan, I know how important this matter is to you. However, since all three of you are injured, would it not be better to leave this matter to me?"

Seo Daeryong's face was full of worry as he looked at her. Dana could feel it. How could she not? Those pure eyes showed his concern for her so plainly.

"Still, I cannot entrust my task to someone so precious."

If this matter was dangerous, would not Seo Daeryong also fall into danger? She could not needlessly drag him into her problem and put him in peril.

"Your body cannot handle it. You must rest."

"I am quite tough, despite appearances. Do not worry."

"I will do it."

Geom Mugeuk, who had been watching the two quarrel, intervened.

"I will handle this matter."

Everyone looked at Geom Mugeuk in surprise. They could understand Seo Daeryong stepping in because of his relationship with Dana, but they had never expected the Young Lord to take on this matter.

Because Cheonhwaru was involved, Geom Mugeuk had to step forward himself.

"I am acquainted with the Lord of Cheonhwaru."

Chohui was startled by those words. But she did not dare ask how he knew the Lord.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to Dana.

"Will you trust me and leave this to me?"

Since Geom Mugeuk said he knew the Lord of Cheonhwaru and offered to take care of it directly, she could not refuse. In truth, she had stubbornly insisted on going herself, but she was inwardly worried about her younger sisters.

But if it was the Young Lord, she could be at ease.

'He is the Young Lord, so the experts of the Demonic Cult will protect him, right?'

Just thinking of those fearsome demonic troops put her mind at ease.

Dana looked at Chohui and asked. Because after all, she was the client.

"Will that be alright?"

Chohui nodded. In any case, if the Demonic Cult had not helped, she would already be dead. She decided to follow the flow of this fate.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the youngest and spoke.

"Youngest Lady, once you treat your internal injuries and see me again, your thoughts will change."

Danyeon smiled. As befitting the youngest, she liked jokes the most among the three sisters. Even now, she knew Geom Mugeuk was joking, but she dared not respond with banter.

She wished that someday, when she was comfortable enough, she could banter with him. She felt they would really get along. But that day would probably never come, would it?

Geom Mugeuk spoke to Seo Daeryong.

"Pavilion Lord, please take the three ladies to the main cult's nearby inner residence and have them treated."

"I will do so."

As Geom Mugeuk walked toward the rear garden to see his father, Seo Daeryong's desperate voice transmission flew to him.

—What should I do when I get there? I'm worried I'll ruin this good relationship! Should I recite another poem? Or not? Please don't leave me alone!

Geom Mugeuk went to the rear garden without answering. Of course, Seo Daeryong was worried.

'When it comes to matters of the heart, I do not trust you.'

But matters of the heart are ultimately a two-way problem. Geom Mugeuk trusted Dana, who had a bold personality.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk relayed to his father what he had heard from Chohui.

"As it is a matter concerning Cheonhwaru, I think I must go myself."

His father knew as well. That the Lord of Cheonhwaru had a deep relationship with Geukak Soma. Because she had visited the main cult in the past to see Geukak Soma.

Geom Mugeuk was regretful. Truly regretful. Because this was the moment the journey with his father, which he had wanted to make last so long, was ending.

"Please return first. I will come back after handling this matter."

No matter how much he enjoyed the time with his father, he could not have his father accompany him to escort Chohui as well. Moreover, his father had been away from the cult for too long.

"This journey was more precious than any journey I have taken since I was born."

His father would probably think of it as a journey of his young life.

It was a statement that included every journey from before his regression.

"I enjoyed the ordinary days I spent with you so much. Eating meals, drinking tea, walking together, fishing. Taking spirit medicines, killing enemies, growing in martial arts. Those ordinary moments were dearer to me than even those moments."

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk bowed his head respectfully.

"Thank you for being with me, Father."

Geom Woojin was looking at his son in silence.

That solemnity lasted only a moment before Geom Mugeuk, raising his head, spilled out words.

"We still haven't gone to that noodle house we said we'd visit together, have we? That place that used to be a dumpling house. So this isn't the end, Father! This is only the first journey. The second journey, the third journey, up to the hundredth journey are all prepared!"

A smirk formed on Geom Woojin's lips. The end of this journey concluded with his father's smirk. Today, that smirk seemed to say this:

'I look forward to the next journey as well.'

"Yes, please look forward to it! I will fill it with even more ordinary, more everyday days."

Geom Mugeuk did not forget to greet Hyeolcheon Doma, who was with them.

"I will see you at the cult, Elder."

Through this journey, he would leave behind his past as a disciple and now live the rest of his life as a martial uncle.

'Elder, if it is Daeryong, that life will have meaning.'

At Geom Mugeuk's heated gaze, Hyeolcheon Doma nodded.

"The moment I return, I will pack my pajamas and come to see you first, Elder, so you must put me up for the night."

And finally, he bid farewell to Hwi.

"Uncle Hwi! Next time, convince Father again so we can go on an outing! Thank you for your hard work driving the carriage!"

Then Hwi revealed himself.

"Please go and return safely."

Geom Woojin and Hyeolcheon Doma looked at Hwi with surprised expressions. He was not one to reveal himself just to say farewell.

Hwi had wanted to say these words personally.

"Thank you, Young Lord."

This journey was one he could never forget for the rest of his life. Thanks to it, his relationship with Geom Woojin had deepened further.

Because it was precious time spent with Hwi, free from concealment.

Because this was a journey that taught him that even this moment of revealing himself was a precious part of his life.

"I enjoyed walking with you, Uncle."

Chapter 531: Even if the Entire Martial World Blocks the Way.
-------------------------------------------------------------
At the branch entrance, they split three ways.

The carriage carrying his father and Hyeolcheon Doma went to the headquarters; Seo Daeryong and the three women went to the private residence; Geom Mugeuk and Chohui went to Cheonhwaru.

Shortly after his father's carriage departed, Jang Ho and the demonic soldiers finished preparing to depart. They would follow his father's carriage and return to the cult.

Before leaving, Geom Mugeuk exchanged farewells with Jang Ho. Jang Ho and the demonic soldiers had played a major role in this matter. After all, he was the one who had saved Seo Daeryong's life.

"Commander Jang."

"Yes, Young Cult Leader."

Their gazes intertwined in the air. Without speaking a word, they both knew well how much the other cherished them.

Geom Mugeuk always felt his spirits lift at the stable feeling filled with loyalty that Jang Ho gave him. Because this man was here, he felt he could entrust anything to him.

Jang Ho would surely understand his own heart from this gaze alone.

Geom Mugeuk's gaze turned to the demonic soldiers standing behind Jang Ho. Today, he conveyed his gratitude to them.

"You all are a wall. An iron wall that protects me, my father, and the cultists of the headquarters. Because that unbreakable wall was there this time as well, I was able to complete the journey safely. I thank you all."

Geom Mugeuk expressed his gratitude with a cupped-hand salute. It was the first time he had formally expressed his thanks in such a manner.

The demonic soldiers all returned the cupped-hand salute in unison with fierce gazes, showing their respect.

"We will devote our full loyalty!"

Jang Ho greeted him one last time.

"Then I shall see you at the headquarters!"

Jang Ho looked after Seo Daeryong until the very end. Because the Dana sisters were watching, he bade farewell to Seo Daeryong even more politely.

"If you come to the headquarters, let's have a drink. My treat."

"I'll send word once I return. Without fail!"

Seo Daeryong emphasized the words "without fail" with an expression that said he truly had much he wanted to say. Since he was so grateful, even if they drank, he should be the one to pay. He would drink with Jang Ho all night long.

Thus the demonic soldiers departed; next, Seo Daeryong departed; and finally, Geom Mugeuk departed.

Only Gang Dal and his subordinates remained. It felt as though a storm had just swept through. The events they had gone through earlier had felt like a dream; rather, now that everyone had left, it felt like a dream.

One of the subordinates spoke while watching the carriage disappear into the distance.

"The branch chief showed us in person how to get to the headquarters, but too many things are needed."

You needed women being pursued, the Hwangcheon Gakju and the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, demonic soldiers and their commander, a Majon and the Cult Leader—for something like that to happen. Ah, and a prepared branch chief too.

"We can't go. So please don't forget and come visit us for sure."

Gang Dal spoke as he went inside.

"We've all worked hard, so today we feast!"

Everyone cheered and went inside.

* * *

"I'll drive the carriage."

She had lived the life of a ranger for a long time, so of course she could drive a carriage well.

"No, it's fine."

When Seo Daeryong strongly refused, Dana eventually sat together on the driver's seat. She couldn't let a high-ranking figure of the Demonic Cult drive the carriage while she sat comfortably in the back.

"You haven't fully recovered from your injuries, so please rest comfortably inside."

"I'm fine. I'll be your talking companion at least."

Seo Daeryong was grateful and happy that she sat beside him. But the problem was that he was too nervous.

Seo Daeryong couldn't even look at her, keeping his eyes forward as he drove the carriage.

What should he talk about? Should he ask about ranger work? Or about her younger sisters? Would she find it annoying if he tried to talk?

All sorts of thoughts flitted through Seo Daeryong's mind. His usually excellent brain was completely useless right now. No, rather, it only produced more distracting thoughts.

Dana was the first to speak to Seo Daeryong.

"You drive the carriage well."

"Yes."

Seo Daeryong smiled shyly. His gaze remained fixed straight ahead.

He was someone who had driven the Cult Leader's carriage, was he not? Displaying the skills he had learned from Hwi, he drove the carriage skillfully. Had Geom Mugeuk seen him, he would have teased that Seo Daeryong drove more carefully than when he drove for his father.

But what good was it to drive the carriage well for nothing? He couldn't even say a word to her.

There were observers watching the two of them.

Had they been outside, it would have been a crisis, but the observers were inside. Through a small window between the driver's seat and the passenger compartment, Danbi and Danyeon were keeping an eye on the two.

The two whispered, expressing their frustration.

"He's just looking ahead and driving."

"He doesn't know the first thing about a woman's heart. If she's sitting beside him, he should at least try to start a conversation."

"Please look to the side!"

Once again, Dana spoke first.

"You seemed close to the Young Cult Leader."

If it had been any other topic, who knew? But he had much to say about Geom Mugeuk.

"He is the one who pulled me out of depression and pessimism. I will serve him loyally even if it costs me my life."

Dana covered the hole at the back of the driver's seat with her hand.

Because the sound of her younger sisters' grumbling was leaking out.

She could guess what they were saying without hearing. That he was telling a woman he had just started with that he would devote his life to someone else. What was so good about a man like that. They would be exchanging such words.

"Perhaps, Miss Dan, do you also have such a person?"

At Seo Daeryong's question, Dana answered.

"Not yet. I'll have to make one from now on, won't I?"

As she said this, Dana looked at Seo Daeryong. Seo Daeryong was still looking ahead, driving the carriage.

Clueless as Seo Daeryong was, the younger sisters understood their older sister's heart.

She definitely had feelings for Seo Daeryong. They weren't the most perceptive either, but a pure person like him didn't seem bad.

But the problem was this.

"He's a demonic human; will that be okay?"

And not just any demonic human, but a high-ranking demonic human at that.

As always, the youngest didn't worry.

"If unni is happy, I'm fine."

Danbi had the most cautious personality among the three, but she too nodded. If it had been any other matter, she would have said absolutely not to get involved with the Demonic Cult. She believed that getting involved with demonic humans ended badly.

But if it was for her older sister's happiness.

Having lost their parents at a young age, their older sister had sacrificed her entire life for her younger sisters.

Because of them, she had never properly dated a man. Whenever a good man appeared, she had avoided him.

"For unni's sake, I could let her go."

Could she really endure a life without seeing her older sister? She couldn't be sure. It would probably be difficult. But if her older sister could be happy, she would gladly choose that hardship.

Then the youngest, Danyeon, spoke.

"Why would we send her away?"

At Danbi's questioning gaze, Danyeon spoke.

"We can just join the Demonic Cult too."

Danbi's expression went blank before she soon smiled. It was something she had never considered before. Yes, her younger sister was right. They could just become demonic humans too.

They looked ahead again through the hole.

"Please look to the side!"

After driving for some time, Seo Daeryong suddenly spoke.

"I am actually shyer and less tactful in dealing with people than I appear."

As if to prove it, Seo Daeryong's gaze remained fixed on the road ahead.

"Thanks to the Young Cult Leader's influence, I've improved a lot just to get to this level. The poem I recited that day was something I had memorized by chance the day before."

He simply told the truth honestly.

Dana gave no response.

"Did I say something unnecessary?"

Just as regret began to well up in Seo Daeryong, Dana spoke calmly.

"I am very tactful, you know."

Surprised, Seo Daeryong looked at her.

"Having lived as a ranger for so long, I've learned too much tact. The Sector Lord might be disappointed in me if he saw."

"No! That wouldn't happen! I like it unconditionally!"

Seeing the excited Seo Daeryong, Dana smiled and spoke.

"Then... please memorize and recite another poem for me."

Seo Daeryong's pupils trembled as he looked at her.

Right then!

Rattle!

Because he had been looking only at her, the carriage failed to avoid a stone on the ground and shook violently.

The two younger sisters shouted from behind.

"Look ahead!"

* * *

The carriage carrying Geom Mugeuk and Chohui raced swiftly along the main road.

The carriage Geom Mugeuk had chosen was a small carriage that could not carry luggage and could only fit two people on the driver's seat, and accordingly, its speed was fast.

After half a day since departure, Chohui reached one conclusion.

"This Young Cult Leader is special."

Having dealt with many men as customers until now, she prided herself on having an eye for men second to none.

Yet there was more than one thing that defied her expectations.

First of all.

He drove the carriage too well. Not just well, but too well.

As if his sole purpose was to get there quickly, Geom Mugeuk focused solely on driving the carriage.

Dududududu.

The carriage left the main road and entered a narrow path. It was truly a path so narrow that a single carriage filled it completely. It was the kind of road where one wrong move would send them plunging into a ditch, yet Geom Mugeuk drove skillfully.

One would think the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult would only ride in luxury carriages driven by subordinates.

Chohui looked down at the side of the carriage. It was running truly no further than a sheet of paper from the edge of the road.

The second special point.

And more surprising than his carriage-driving skill was his sense of direction.

Geom Mugeuk knew the roads too well. He would weave off and merge back again. If there was a shortcut, he would definitely take it to shorten the time.

"Is this a road he travels every day?"

She thought to that extent. But surely the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult wouldn't have driven a carriage down this road every day?

"Is he really the Young Cult Leader?"

This was the reason for such doubt, and at the same time, the third special point.

"We're going just the two of us, right?"

While racing along like this, she hadn't seen any guard warriors following. She thought that even if they wanted to chase, they couldn't, having passed through all sorts of side paths.

"Yes, it's just us."

It was the expected answer, but Chohui looked at Geom Mugeuk with surprised eyes. She knew this person was no ordinary man, but really just the two of them?

"I like traveling alone normally too."

"Still, I thought you would at least bring a few guards. You are a nobleman."

"It is my father's philosophy. The more noble you are, the less you should spoil yourself. Raise yourself to feel your shortcomings."

Of course, some parents might raise their children that way. But you are the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult?

The fourth special point.

It was Geom Mugeuk's attitude toward her.

If he had known she was a gisaeng, his speech or eyes might have revealed a hint of contempt. Yet from the beginning until now, the Young Cult Leader had not lost his courtesy.

The more special the Young Cult Leader seemed, the greater her suspicions grew. She had seen the Cheonmado and the Majon at the branch.

"Are you really the Young Cult Leader? A Young Cult Leader escorting alone? And the Cult Leader permits that?"

Doubt begat more doubt.

"Could he be using schemes to extract the information I hold?"

Yes, there was no harm in being careful. Considering the importance of the information she possessed.

Chohui hid such negative feelings and spoke with a bright expression.

"Well. You don't need guards, do you? Who could attack us when you're driving through such complicated roads?"

"They could."

"What?"

"We're not alone right now."

Chohui made an expression asking what he meant. Then astounding words flowed from Geom Mugeuk's mouth.

"There is someone tailing us. They have been following us since we left the branch."

Chohui was startled.

"They are skilled in pursuit techniques."

The opponent possessed such skill that unless one were a considerable expert, they wouldn't realize they were being followed.

"Then does that mean they know the Young Cult Leader is taking me?"

"That may be, or it may not."

He had been monitoring the outskirts of the branch and then set out in pursuit. The branch where his father, Hyeolcheon Doma, and the demonic soldiers had taken positions earlier was not a situation where someone could infiltrate and assess things.

Monitoring from outside like that, when Chohui came out, he began tailing the carriage she rode.

Chohui felt that the danger had not yet ended.

Then the opponent could send people even stronger than the Chilro Chusaldde.

Realizing she was exposed to even greater danger, her nerves were on edge.

"Aren't you curious? About what I know?"

Given the situation, he should be urging her to reveal everything she knew.

"I am curious."

"Then why don't you ask?"

"If you could reveal it, you would have already told me."

Geom Mugeuk looked at her. His clear, deep eyes seemed to look into her heart.

"I know that Miss Cho is no ordinary gisaeng."

For a moment, Chohui flinched in surprise.

"What ordinary gisaeng would hire rangers to protect herself? What gisaeng would try to keep secrets in a situation where demonic humans are swarming? You must certainly hold special meaning to the Tower Master of Cheonhwaru. Or you are acting on his orders."

Chohui said nothing.

Geom Mugeuk asked her in a calm tone.

"Do you remember when I said I was acquainted with the Tower Master of Cheonhwaru?"

Chohui nodded.

"Then now it is time for you to ask me. How close I am to the Tower Master of Cheonhwaru."

She asked him using his own words.

"How close are you to the Tower Master?"

Then unexpected words flowed from Geom Mugeuk's mouth.

"Even if the entire martial world blocks this road, I will take you to the Tower Master. I am exactly that close to him."

"!"

He could get hurt or nearly die fighting alongside the Geukak Soma.

But the Tower Master of Cheonhwaru must not.

Because she was that Soma's woman.

Only then did Chohui understand. That the reason Geom Mugeuk was taking her was because of the Tower Master of Cheonhwaru. She let out a sigh of relief. Her greatest doubt disappeared with this.

"Then that is fortunate. What I must convey is information the Tower Master absolutely needs."

Chohui added with a cautious gaze.

"So even if the entire martial world blocks us, please make sure to take me there."

Chapter 532: The Most Famous Mask in the Martial World
------------------------------------------------------
The carriage kept racing onward.

There was no leisure to admire the scenery. Trees and flowers whose names she did not know flashed past, and the smell of dust pricked at the tip of her nose.

They said the tail was still on them, but even so, Chohui’s heart was more at ease than at first.

Because she could feel that when he had said he would take her to the Master of Cheonhwaru even if the entire Murim tried to stop him, he had meant it. After all, she was better than anyone at seeing through a man’s lies.

Her gaze flicked toward Geom Mugeuk, who was driving the carriage.

The more she looked at him, the more extraordinary he seemed.

‘Just what sort of story does he have with the Tower Master?’

She was curious about the relationship between this man and her master.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk abruptly spoke.

“They must be getting impatient.”

“Pardon?”

At that instant, Chohui’s body flew into the air. Geom Mugeuk had wrapped an arm around her waist and leapt upward.

Suddenly carried into the air by Geom Mugeuk, Chohui could not gather her wits. The sound of hidden weapons slicing past rang by her ears.

Swish, swish, swish, swish, swish!

Sounds of something embedding itself.

Thud, thud, thud, thud, thud!

Masked men, sky, trees, and earth flashed through her vision.

Shwaaaaaak!

It was her first time hearing the sound of sword qi being released from a blade. And from this close, no less.

Thump.

When she came to her senses, she was sitting in the carriage, which had somehow come to a stop.

Turning her head, she saw masked men collapsed all around them. She had not seen how they died. Everything had simply happened and ended in an instant.

Geom Mugeuk was crouched down, examining their corpses.

As she was about to get down from the carriage, Geom Mugeuk spoke without looking back.

“Be careful when you get off. They’re coated in poison.”

As if to prove that what had just happened had not been a dream, several hidden weapons with blue-gleaming edges were stuck into the side of the carriage.

When she thought that dozens of such terrifying things had flown through the air, and that she and the Young Cult Leader had flown between them, her heart sank belatedly.

Afraid for no reason that her foot might slip as she climbed down and she might cut herself on a hidden weapon, she sat back down.

“This time, they hired assassins.”

Geom Mugeuk had already recognized them as assassins from the movements they used to attack him. Even so, he examined the corpses to find out where they were from. But he could not find a single trace.

“The one trying to kill you is thoroughly concealing his identity.”

First they had hired wandering blades, and now assassins. The Chillo Pursuit Killers had been the same, but these assassins were also highly skilled.

If they had the wealth to hire people of this caliber, they should have reliable experts of their own. Yet they were trying to settle matters by borrowing blades from outside. And by using people who were almost impossible to trace.

After he had finished examining all the corpses, Geom Mugeuk returned to the carriage.

Chohui could understand that the reason Geom Mugeuk was leaving the tail alive was to draw out information about the other side.

‘Wouldn’t it be better to kill the one following us, avoid the attacks, and arrive even a little sooner?’

The thought came to her, but she did not ask. Since she had entrusted everything to Geom Mugeuk, she had to believe in him. Even if she did not know what it was that he believed in.

Before getting into the carriage, Geom Mugeuk extended his hand, and the hidden weapons embedded in the carriage pulled themselves free. Geom Mugeuk buried the poison-coated weapons deep in the ground.

It was another moment in which Chohui discovered something extraordinary about him. An ordinary martial artist would have simply pulled them out and tossed them aside. But he was worried that if he left them on a road like this, someone who picked them up unknowingly might die.

The feeling that this was not hypocrisy confused her. After all, the other party was the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.

“Thank you for saving me.”

Chohui bowed her head and expressed her gratitude politely.

“Think nothing of it. Then we’ll be off.”

He could have easily made some half-joking show of taking credit, but Geom Mugeuk simply began driving the carriage again.

Chohui quietly stared at Geom Mugeuk, then took a deep breath and steadied herself. She never knew when she might suddenly fly into the sky again.

The second ambush took place two nights later.

Chohui was sleeping beside the campfire.

How long had she slept?

Without knowing why she had woken, she opened her eyes. And she was startled.

Blood was dripping down onto her body. Before her eyes was a masked man. His dagger was aimed at her throat.

And Geom Mugeuk’s sword had reached first, buried in his heart.

Geom Mugeuk pushed the man’s body aside, making it collapse next to her.

“He was an assassin with remarkable concealment and infiltration skills.”

Geom Mugeuk examined the corpse. He tried to find out which organization had sent him, but the assassin had not a single tattoo or scar on his body.

She bowed her head politely.

“You’ve saved me again.”

What had saved her was the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art. No matter how faint the presence, it could not deceive the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art that had reached great completion.

“You must be awake now, but sleep some more.”

Geom Mugeuk buried the assassin’s corpse.

Chohui could tell. Geom Mugeuk had not buried the bodies of their enemies before, and the reason he was burying this one today was for her sake. He could not have her sleep beside a corpse.

After sitting for a while and staring at the campfire, she brought up something she had never spoken of before.

“The Tower Master created an intelligence organization within Cheonhwaru. It is a pleasure house and, at the same time, an intelligence organization.”

When people became drunk, all sorts of words spilled out. In that respect, there was no place better than a pleasure house for drawing out information about the Murim.

“Of course, not all courtesans handle information. The courtesans who deal with intelligence are called Secret Flowers. I am also a Secret Flower.”

It was a secret she revealed as a sign that she now trusted him, the Young Cult Leader. She had resolved herself greatly before disclosing it, but Geom Mugeuk’s reaction was unexpectedly calm.

“Did you perhaps already know?”

At her question, Geom Mugeuk nodded. He had already known. He knew that the Master of Cheonhwaru had been gathering information about the Murim for Geukak Soma. He had simply not known the specific detail that they were called Secret Flowers.

Now she could be certain. This Young Cult Leader truly had a deep connection to Cheonhwaru.

“Go to sleep. We’ll leave early tomorrow.”

“Yes.”

A short while later, lying with her back turned, she asked,

“The Tower Master will be all right, won’t she?”

Since they were so persistently trying to kill her, she suddenly worried for the Master of Cheonhwaru’s safety. Of course, there were experts protecting the Tower Master, but what if an assassin as skilled as the one from earlier went after her?

She had expected him to say she would be fine, but Geom Mugeuk’s answer came like a bolt from the blue.

“They will probably try to kill the Tower Master as well. They may already have tried.”

Chohui shot upright in surprise.

“Do you truly think so?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“If they cannot kill the person trying to deliver the information, would they not simply kill the person meant to receive it?”

“!”

For a moment, Chohui turned pale and could not say a word.

Then she cried out loudly.

“Please go quickly and save the Tower Master!”

She was usually calm by nature, but when her master’s safety was at stake, she became agitated. As Geom Mugeuk guessed it, she valued the Tower Master more than her own life.

“Please! Please go and save the Tower Master! You said you were close enough to take me to her even if the entire Murim tried to stop you!”

Compared to her, Geom Mugeuk remained calm.

“That’s right. I did.”

“Then please go at once! If it’s because of me, don’t worry. I’ll hide somewhere and wait. Please go quickly and save her.”

Only then did Geom Mugeuk reveal why he had been so calm.

“You do not need to worry. I have already made preparations.”

“Preparations?”

Her startled eyes trembled.

“She is that precious to me. Do you think I would have simply left her be? I am not such an optimistic person.”

If he had not prepared a countermeasure, he would have carried her on his back and run with swift footwork.

“The day I heard that you were trying to meet the Master of Cheonhwaru, I immediately sent an urgent message. So we need only focus on our own task.”

“To whom?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at her and smiled meaningfully.

“There is someone. Someone I always miss, no matter where they are or what they are doing.”

* * *

A group of martial artists arrived at the main headquarters of Cheonhwaru in Guizhou.

Their movements were nimble. Just as one could tell from their lightness skill alone that their martial ability was far from ordinary, their cold eyes revealed their cruel dispositions.

They were the ones known to be the most merciless among wandering blades.

They were the commissioned mercenary group made up of former assassins, the Gwisaldae.

True to their origins as assassins, they had not a shred of hesitation when killing people. They were cold-blooded men who would cut down a child if a child stood in their way, and cut down parents if parents stood in their way. They were called Gwisal, Ghost Killers, because it was said that if you paid them, they would even kill a ghost for you.

If the Chillo Pursuit Killers occupied the dark side among wandering blades who chased people down, then among wandering blades who killed people for hire, the Gwisaldae held that place.

The man standing before them was the commander who led the Ghost Killers. Said to have succeeded in all fifty assassinations he carried out during his days as an assassin, he was a man at the very top in both skill and cruelty.

“Kill them all, courtesans and guests alike! Do not let a single one leave alive!”

Even at their commander’s order, the Ghost Killers did not emit the slightest trace of killing intent. Nor did they look as though they were looking forward to the slaughter that was about to unfold.

To them, who were thoroughly accustomed to killing people, what was about to take place was simply work. It was no different from ordinary people carrying loads or selling goods.

One of the Ghost Killers looked at the building lit with brilliant lights and said,

“It’s perfect music for the road to the underworld!”

Whether Cheonhwaru knew of the invasion of these cruel men or not, lively music drifted out from the building.

The Ghost Killers vaulted over the wall all at once.

One might have expected at least a courtesan greeting guests or an old servant to be there, but the courtyard happened to be empty.

Because of that, they ran swiftly without being noticed by anyone and entered the building.

The first floor of Cheonhwaru had rooms for guests on all sides, centered around a space like a plaza.

“We sweep from the first floor upward!”

At the Ghost Killer commander’s order, the Ghost Killers flung open the first door.

Just as they were about to rush in and butcher everyone inside, the Ghost Killer who had opened the door flinched in surprise.

The instant the door opened, a bizarre sight entered his eyes.

Inside sat men wearing white masks.

‘What the hell? Who are these crazy bastards?’

The white masks had only eyeholes cut into them. They bore smiling expressions like crescent moons laid on their sides. Each crescent differed slightly in shape.

The reason the Ghost Killer was startled was that the eyes behind those smiling eyeholes were not smiling. The eeriness born of that dissonance.

“What are they? Those things?”

At the words of another Ghost Killer behind him, the one at the front said,

“Those are the masks worn by the Faceless Guests.”

At the words Faceless Guests, everyone’s expressions stiffened.

But soon, someone laughed.

“Insane.”

The most famous masks in the Murim were the masks of the Faceless Guests. Masks sold in marketplaces, masks that inevitably appeared whenever children played sword fights, were Faceless Guest masks.

“Is today some kind of Faceless Guest company dinner?”

The laughter spread to everyone. Naturally, they assumed they were not the real Faceless Guests.

The Faceless Guests were famous for never taking off their masks in front of others. There was no way Faceless Guests would come to a pleasure house like this.

“If they wanted to come here, they should’ve cut mouth holes, not eyeholes!”

“Courtesans won’t even take you lot, will they?”

Just then, the Ghost Killer commander coldly stopped them from behind.

“Enough!”

At that one word, the laughter vanished from the Ghost Killers’ faces. They were just about to rush in and hack them to pieces when—

Srrrk.

The door right beside them opened.

Normally, a drunken guest should have come out supported by a courtesan.

The Ghost Killers were startled again.

Men wearing white masks sat there as well.

This time, no careless words came out.

Even seeing them standing there with swords drawn, not one of those men was shaken.

Instead, the other side sat silently, displaying a presence that overwhelmed them. The eyes within the hollow eyeholes were all smiling.

‘What the hell are these freaks?’

It was chilling and unpleasant, but even then, they had still thought, surely not.

Srrk, srrk, srrrk.

Doors began to open one after another. As the doors around them opened in succession, an astonishing sight unfolded.

In every room sat masked figures.

The music that had been resounding suddenly ceased.

In the silence, the masked Faceless Guests all looked at the Ghost Killers at once. Because the expressionless masks had only eyeholes cut into them, the mere sight of them staring was indescribably bizarre and eerie.

The Ghost Killers gathered in the center. Overwhelmed by the other side’s presence, they had unknowingly retreated. Since they had begun operating under the name Gwisaldae, this was the first time they had ever felt such fear.

The Ghost Killer commander drew his sword. A fight, in the end, was all about momentum.

“There is no way they are the real Faceless Guests. Kill every bastard pretending to be demons!”

At that very instant—

Whoosh!

A cool rush of wind brushed past their ears.

At the same time, a dull thud followed.

The Ghost Killers looked toward the source of the sound.

In that instant, their eyes widened in shock.

Their gazes had gathered on the Ghost Killer commander.

The Ghost Killer commander looked back at them with an expression that seemed to ask why they were looking at him.

Drip.

Hot liquid ran down from his forehead over his face.

The Ghost Killer commander touched his forehead. There was a hole in it.

“Shiiit!”

Leaving behind only that one word, the Ghost Killer commander toppled backward and died.

The Ghost Killers were aghast. An attack had flown in that the Ghost Killer commander, with his skill, had not even had time to attempt to dodge? They could not believe it even after seeing it.

Who on earth? The horrified Ghost Killers looked around. The white masks facing them were all smiling.

Then one of the Ghost Killers pointed somewhere with a trembling voice.

“……There!”

Far in the distance, there was one room whose door alone had not opened.

In that closed door, a single hole had been punched.

Chapter 533: Looks Better at Talking Than Martial Arts.
-------------------------------------------------------
A single word slipped from one of the Gwisal.

“……The Extreme Evil Young Demon!”

The Gwisal understood.

These were the real Faceless Guests, and the person in that room was the Extreme Evil Young Demon.

At such a distance, with the door shut besides, the only person who could kill the leader of the Gwisal Squad in a single move was the Extreme Evil Young Demon.

The martial world feared the Demon Venerables, but the ones they feared especially were the Poison King, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, and the Extreme Evil Young Demon.

The reason for fearing each was different.

The Poison King, because no matter how many people charged at him, they could be massacred without even getting the chance to lift a hand. The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, because one might die while cutting down one’s own comrades in a state that was not one’s own will.

These two Demon Venerables were feared more by the righteous faction than by the evil faction. It was not death itself they feared, but the helplessness of the process.

The Extreme Evil Young Demon, on the other hand, was feared more by the villains of the evil faction. They knew better than anyone what terrifying deeds they themselves could commit. And the Extreme Evil Young Demon stood at the very pinnacle of that evil, a being bearing the name of extreme wickedness.

As if by agreement, three of the Gwisal hurled themselves toward the entrance at the same time. They tried to flee without even looking back.

Whoosh!

Another finger wind came flying.

A single streak of finger wind followed them. Since there were three fleeing, it seemed as if it meant to kill only one of them.

Slaaaash!

At the final instant, the finger wind split into three strands.

Thud! Thump! Thud!

With their neck, head, and heart pierced through, the three Gwisal rolled across the floor.

One of the Gwisal pointed at the door again.

“……There!”

Everyone’s gaze turned toward the door, still closed. It was astonishing enough that he had struck without seeing, and astonishing too that a single streak of finger wind had split at the last moment.

But the most astonishing fact was this.

There was still only one hole.

He had fired the finger wind again through the hole that had killed the leader of the Gwisal Squad, killing the three of them. In other words, it had curved through the air as it chased them down.

They felt helplessness and terror at the same time. That one move made the stubborn desire to clench their teeth and survive gutter out in an instant.

Originally, those were emotions their opponents should have felt toward them. It was the fear the courtesans and guests who were meant to die here should have felt.

Sssrk.

Then, all at once, the Faceless Guests in the rooms rose from their seats.

The terrified Gwisal were forced to feel a new fear. The Faceless Guests who had risen were all pointing their fingers at them.

“Shit!”

As someone’s despairing curse gave the signal.

Shukshukshukshukshukshukshukshukshukshukshukshuk!

Finger winds poured toward them from every direction.

Lines wove together like a spiderweb.

The finger wind that rained down was the Evil Shadow Finger (惡影指), the technique practiced by the Faceless Guests. Once they formally became successors, they would learn the Extreme Evil Young Demon’s Blood Calamity Finger.

The Faceless Guests who had come here today were among the most skilled even in the Valley of Villains. That was why the Gwisal, having lost all will to fight, could not block their attacks pouring in from every direction.

Thudthudthudthudthudthudthudthud!

All the remaining Gwisal collapsed, their bodies riddled with holes like hedgehogs.

It was the moment the infamous hired ronin band of former assassins, the Gwisal Squad, vanished from the world.

Only then did the last closed door begin to open slowly.

There sat the mistress of Cheonhwaru, who had been the Gwisal’s target today.

She still exuded that strange atmosphere that could only be felt from her. A mysterious insight, as if she could see through a person’s fate, flowed in her calm eyes.

Beside her sat someone who contrasted with her, and yet suited her well all the same.

The Extreme Evil Young Demon, smiling in white.

There were many masks in this space, but the man beside the mistress of Cheonhwaru revealed an overwhelming presence.

His gaze had grown even deeper than before, and his martial realm had risen another level.

The Blood Calamity Finger he had shown just now was no longer the same Blood Calamity Finger as before.

Originally, it had been a single streak of finger wind, but now he could divide it into several branches.

The ten-thousand-year snow ginseng Geom Mugeuk had given him had greatly increased his internal energy, and an even greater reason was that there had recently been a change in his state of mind.

In his room, where there had been nothing but white walls, a bed had been placed, and a desk and chair as well.

Only when life changes does martial arts change.

He, too, was in the midst of being subject to that great principle of martial learning.

Not only the Blood Calamity Finger, but his other exclusive martial art, the Demonic Extreme Berserk Palm, was also showing a different form from before.

The mistress of Cheonhwaru poured wine into the Extreme Evil Young Demon’s cup. He would not remove his mask to drink in a place like this, but because of that, the gesture held even more meaning.

For she was the only woman who poured wine for the Extreme Evil Young Demon.

“The smell of blood will seep in.”

At the Extreme Evil Young Demon’s words, the mistress of Cheonhwaru smiled and said,

“The scent of rouge from women erases the smell of blood quickly enough.”

How could the Extreme Evil Young Demon not know?

In the martial world, the more dangerous scent was not the smell of blood, but the scent of women’s rouge.

The mistress of Cheonhwaru had everyone upstairs come down.

The courtesans and everyone working in the brothel came down to the lower floor.

Before the bodies were cleared away, the mistress of Cheonhwaru expressed her gratitude first.

She rose from her seat and stepped forward. Standing before the Extreme Evil Young Demon, she bowed with utmost courtesy. Since very few people knew the relationship between the two of them, this was now a very public situation.

“I will not forget the grace of the Demon Venerable in saving my life.”

The courtesans who had come down stood in a circle and bowed to the Extreme Evil Young Demon and the Faceless Guests all around.

“Thank you for saving us.”

If the Extreme Evil Young Demon had not come, everyone here would have died.

The Extreme Evil Young Demon merely gave a single nod.

“Clear away the bodies.”

At the mistress of Cheonhwaru’s command, the men who worked in the brothel began to remove the corpses. The courtesans brought buckets and rags and began wiping away the blood. They had every right to be frightened, yet the courtesans of the main branch, who had been through all manner of hardship, were stouthearted.

The Extreme Evil Young Demon poured her a cup of wine.

After drinking, the mistress of Cheonhwaru spoke softly enough that only the Extreme Evil Young Demon could hear.

“Thank you, Orabeoni.”

“I was able to come because the Young Cult Master sent word. Offer your thanks when the Young Cult Master arrives.”

The mistress of Cheonhwaru smiled and said,

“Even so, the one who came running and saved me was not the Young Cult Master, but you, Orabeoni.”

The two eyes inside the eyeholes smiled.

Of course she was grateful to Geom Mugeuk as well. It was a message that had saved her life, so how could she not be grateful? She had only said that to convey a greater gratitude to the Extreme Evil Young Demon.

And how could that be the only reason she was grateful to Geom Mugeuk?

The look in the Extreme Evil Young Demon’s eyes as he gazed at her had changed. It did not mean that his gaze had become more affectionate toward her. She could tell from his eyes that the person called the Extreme Evil Young Demon himself had changed.

She felt that this change was altering the Extreme Evil Young Demon’s fate. And that change did not feel bad at all.

“If they went so far as to mobilize the Gwisal Squad, it means you touched some very wealthy bastards.”

At the Extreme Evil Young Demon’s words, the mistress of Cheonhwaru quietly nodded.

Recently, she had continued expanding Cheonhwaru throughout the Central Plains. At the same time, she had been training quick-witted and clever courtesans into Secret Flowers.

She did not yet know whether this incident had arisen because of Cheonhwaru’s expansion or because of information the Secret Flowers had obtained. She would know only after Chohui arrived.

“The Young Cult Master will be all right, won’t he?”

The Extreme Evil Young Demon nodded.

“There is absolutely no need to worry about that side.”

Yeojeong smiled as she spoke. To the end, she took care of her man’s pride and morale.

“It’s this side I’m not worried about at all.”

In the meantime, all the bodies had been cleared away, and all the blood had been wiped clean.

The courtesans and workers all went back upstairs.

Srrk! Srrrk! Srrrk!

The doors to every courtesan’s room began closing one after another.

The Extreme Evil Young Demon’s room, which had opened last, was the last to close.

As if nothing had happened, lively music began to sound there once more.

* * *

Not far from Cheonhwaru, a carriage stood still.

The person inside the carriage was Seo Danmyeong (徐旦明).

He was the architect of this pursuit and ambush.

Then his right-hand man, Jaein (才人), climbed into the carriage and said,

“The Gwisal who entered Cheonhwaru are not coming out.”

Seo Danmyeong’s gaze turned to Jaein, then returned to the distant building of Cheonhwaru.

No matter how Cheonhwaru was the greatest brothel of the age, expanding its branches throughout the Central Plains, he had been certain it could not stop the Gwisal Squad.

“Are we facing invincible courtesans or something?”

Ordinarily, Jaein would have laughed at Seo Danmyeong’s joke even if it were not funny, but today he could not. The situation was extremely bad.

“We have also lost contact with those who went in to investigate.”

Of course, Seo Danmyeong knew very well why this matter was not going smoothly.

“Did the Demonic Cult send support?”

“It seems that way.”

“They shouldn’t have had enough time to prepare.”

“They moved faster than we did.”

Which meant the other side had made the correct judgment.

“When I received the commission to kill a courtesan, I had a bad feeling from the start.”

At Seo Danmyeong’s regret, Jaein wanted to ask.

‘Then why did you accept it? Why on earth are you pushing this matter so recklessly?’

This time, Seo Danmyeong asked about the situation on Chohui’s side.

“In any case, nothing good ever comes from getting tangled up with those Demonic Cult bastards. So? When will she arrive?”

“If fast, tomorrow night. If late, the day after tomorrow.”

“We have no time.”

He had not expected the assassins to fail in their ambushes one after another. He had been told that the person escorting her was one young man.

Of course, he had assumed that person was an expert of the Demonic Cult. He had not suspected for a moment that it would be the Young Cult Master of the Demonic Cult. There was no way the Young Cult Master would escort a courtesan alone.

“What do you think?”

“I think we should withdraw from this matter even now.”

“If we do that, my reputation will hit rock bottom. No one will entrust us with work again.”

He was not an ordinary hired ronin.

Seo Danmyeong called himself a Netweaver.

A person who wove nets.

One who made thorough plans and arranged affairs.

He did not merely kill people. He persuaded people, mediated between two sects in conflict, and resolved and cleaned up incidents and accidents. Of course, as with this mission, he also killed people.

That was why he called his work art.

He did not move personally.

When tracking someone, he hired martial artists who specialized in pursuit and entrusted the work to them; when someone had to be killed, he used assassin organizations and ronin to handle it.

This was what clients who entrusted work to him found convenient.

They did not have to deal directly with rough and brutal assassins or ronin.

Also, once the commission price was set, even if he failed any number of times afterward, he would see the job through to the end. Of course, the price was extremely high from the start.

No matter what happened, he always achieved his objective. The only thing needed was an appropriate amount of money.

Jaein, his right-hand man, knew Seo Danmyeong was not the sort of person to take on a job to track down and kill a mere courtesan. He must have received the job from someone he could not refuse, or he had been paid an enormous sum he could not turn down.

“Who exactly asked this of you?”

Jaein usually worked silently as Seo Danmyeong ordered, but this time he could not help being curious.

As if understanding Jaein’s feelings, Seo Danmyeong did not get angry and answered only with this.

“Someone who absolutely does not want their identity revealed.”

Jaein was curious about the client, but Seo Danmyeong said no more.

“I have invited one person in preparation for the worst. In the end, this job will leave us deeply in the red.”

Which meant he had prepared a martial artist whose fee was truly exorbitant. Who could it be? Fortunately, that curiosity could be answered.

“This time there will be no mistake, so go with him.”

* * *

“By tomorrow, we should arrive at Cheonhwaru.”

At the thought that she would finally be able to see the pavilion mistress, Chohui’s heart pounded.

“It’s all thanks to you, Young Cult Master. I don’t know how I can ever repay this grace.”

Geom Mugeuk answered with a smile.

“You have already repaid it.”

“Pardon?”

Of course, the choice to visit the minor branch had been Dana’s decision, but in any case, because they had come to the main cult, they had been able to meet him, and as a result, he had been able to send word to the Extreme Evil Young Demon.

Just then, far down the road, two people were blocking the way.

Geom Mugeuk slowed the carriage.

They were two men, and the younger one was Jaein.

Jaein looked at Geom Mugeuk, then calmly said to the man beside him,

“You only need to kill the man. I will handle the woman.”

He might be an expert of the Demonic Cult who had dealt with the assassins, but it felt like using a butcher’s knife to kill a rabbit. Jaein had never imagined this man would have been prepared.

“You mean that fellow who looks better with words than with martial arts?”

Jaein was inwardly puzzled.

‘Better with words? Why would he say that?’

Could someone of this level see through even such inner qualities? But this was not a matter of discerning good and evil in a person.

“How did you know he was talkative?”

Chapter 534: Through the Young Cult Leader It Becomes Routine
-------------------------------------------------------------
Jaein stared intently at Geom Mugeuk’s face.

‘That face looks talkative?’

Jaein was surprised that the other man was far younger and more handsome than he had imagined, but the man beside him was seeing something he could not.

“Look at those eyes.”

Jaein did as the man said and looked into Geom Mugeuk’s eyes.

They were clear and deep, not at all like a demonic practitioner’s.

“Are those not the eyes of a meddler who looks as if he would stick his nose into every affair in the murim?”

“……Is that so?”

“For instance, the very wicked eyes of someone who would lead the perfectly fine heir of the Sado Alliance onto a dance floor.”

Why was the heir of the Sado Alliance suddenly coming up? Dance? What dance?

Jaein looked at the man with a puzzled expression. He had not known this person was so eccentric. In his heart, he wanted to say, “Stop spouting nonsense and hurry up and kill him,” but this was not someone he dared say such a thing to.

“Now that I hear it, he does look very talkative and wicked.”

When Jaein humored him, the man laughed loudly as if a weight had been lifted from his chest.

Then Geom Mugeuk, who had only been listening, asked Chohui beside him.

“What do you think? While traveling with me until now, have I talked a lot?”

“No. You have been taciturn.”

Even as she answered, Chohui was tense. Unlike before, the fact that the opponent had so openly blocked their path meant he must be confident in his abilities.

Yet the two men were exchanging only an inexplicable conversation.

“Did you hear that? I am originally a taciturn person. But everyone is so terribly bad at speaking. They should just say what they want to say, but they swallow it down. Put it off until later. It gets so frustrating that I cannot help but speak, can I? They say silence is the best way to avoid misunderstandings, but is everyone not being misunderstood precisely because they do not speak properly? Take the Young Alliance Leader, for example. When he is not speaking, does he not give off the impression that he is pondering whom to kill? Yet when you actually hear him speak, how adorable he is. And…….”

“Enough!”

At the man’s shout, Geom Mugeuk shut his mouth.

Jaein was inwardly flustered by this unexpected situation. He had never dreamed that he would come here with this man to kill that fellow, only to end up having this sort of conversation. And who was this Young Alliance Leader they kept mentioning?

In any case, at least one thing was certain.

‘He really is a talkative one!’

Jaein cautiously said to the man,

“Please kill that talkative man at once and shut his mouth.”

At that, the man let out a light sigh and said,

“That will not do.”

Jaein was shocked by the words that it would not do.

“What do you mean?”

The one who answered was Geom Mugeuk.

“We know each other, you see. We are even close.”

“Close, my foot!”

The man was none other than Goeak, one of the Seven Great Masters of the Unorthodox Path.

The last time they had met was at the tripartite talks, and now they had encountered each other again like this.

Rather, from Goeak’s attitude of denial, Jaein could sense it.

‘The two of them are close!’

Jaein was overcome with dismay. Just who in the world was that young man?

“What are you doing here?”

“What else? I came to kill you.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“Then kill me.”

Goeak quietly stared at Geom Mugeuk.

“It has become even harder to kill you now.”

He had realized from Geom Mugeuk’s eyes and aura that his abilities had risen even further.

“I was asked to kill just one demonic practitioner. I was promised enough money to spend extravagantly for the rest of my life and still have some left. What a pity. It was my chance to become rich.”

Those words were not true.

The reason he had taken this job was because of Geom Mugeuk. If the target was a demonic practitioner formidable enough that he himself had to step forward, he thought it might be someone deeply connected to Geom Mugeuk. He had wanted to see whom these people were trying to kill. He had intended to stop it in advance.

But he truly had never dreamed that the demonic practitioner would be Geom Mugeuk.

“Escorting a courtesan alone? Truly like you.”

Goeak asked Jaein,

“Do you know who that man with the talkative-looking face is?”

“Who is he?”

“He is the Young Cult Leader of the Divine Cult.”

“!”

Jaein was startled out of his wits. The young expert escorting a courtesan was the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult? That talkative man? Impossible!

But there was not the slightest trace of falsehood in Goeak’s expression as he looked at him. Nor was there any reason for him to have followed them all this way just to lie.

“No matter how good money is, surely you were not asking me to kill the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult?”

Only then did Jaein understand. Why this affair had gone so badly. Why there had been so many loose holes in the net of Gyeolmangja, who could carry out even far more difficult tasks like works of art.

Jaein respectfully cupped his fist in greeting.

“I failed to recognize an honored person.”

His chest grew tight and his mind became complicated, but he struggled to remain calm. The one fortunate point was that the Young Cult Leader was truly young.

‘I am someone learning from Gyeolmangja how to design conspiracies. Stay calm.’

Youth was always bound to be accompanied by immaturity and mistakes. There would surely be an opening.

Because Goeak could guess what he was thinking, he inwardly pitied him.

‘He is a man who casts nets ten times, a hundred times larger than the ones you cast. If you knew who was caught in that net, you would never have done such a thing in the first place.’

Geom Mugeuk asked Jaein in a gentle tone,

“Was it you who planned this affair?”

Because his attitude did not look like that of a demonic practitioner no matter how Jaein looked at him, Jaein still could not believe that the other man was the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.

“I am still learning.”

“From whom?”

Just then, as Jaein hesitated for a moment before answering,

Goeak said to Geom Mugeuk,

“There is a person called Gyeolmangja.”

Jaein had not expected Goeak to reveal the mastermind so readily.

Jaein tried to provoke Goeak and find a way to survive.

“Considering the elder, would it not go against propriety for you to do this?”

He had said it in hopes of steering things so that Goeak would spare him, if only because he felt sorry about that point.

Goeak looked at him and laughed.

“My name contains the character for ‘strange,’ and it also contains the character for ‘evil.’ Are you trying to discuss propriety with someone like me? And with Gyeolmangja, ours is a relationship of money, not propriety.”

From those words, Jaein could be certain. Goeak would absolutely not attack the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.

“Whom did you send to Cheonhwaru?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Jaein flinched for an instant.

“How did you know?”

“I asked whom you sent.”

His eyes and tone were calm, but Jaein felt fear. After all, they had tried to annihilate the main branch of Cheonhwaru, where the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult was trying to take the courtesan.

“I sent the Gwisaldae.”

At the words Gwisaldae, Chohui’s heart sank. It was a name she had heard many times through the guests. Every time she heard of the deeds committed under that name, she had frowned and felt afraid. And now that Gwisaldae had gone to the Tower Lord.

Compared to her pounding heart, Geom Mugeuk merely nodded once and asked nothing more.

“Why do you not ask about the result?”

The instant Jaein resolved to use Cheonhwaru as a hostage and survive this place,

“Is it not because they failed that you tried to mobilize even Senior Goe here?”

Only then could Jaein feel it. Though the other man was asking gently, he had seen through both him and the situation.

“Do you want to live?”

“I do. No, I wish to live.”

Jaein behaved as respectfully as he could.

“Who is the mastermind who commissioned this affair?”

“He did not tell me. That is the truth!”

“Where is Gyeolmangja now?”

He could not answer. If he betrayed Gyeolmangja, he would truly suffer extreme agony and die miserably. The Gyeolmangja he knew was that kind of person. He was a man who first wove a tight net to ensnare traitors.

“Gyeolmangja frightens you, but I do not?”

“That is not it.”

“Then again, at times like this, we need our Young Alliance Leader’s face. If you were before him, you would not look down on me like this.”

Bi Sa-in, who was not present, kept being brought up.

“But fortunately, someone even more frightening than the Young Alliance Leader has come to Cheonhwaru.”

Jaein’s eyes widened as if asking what that meant.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk sent out a finger wind, sealing his internal energy, and pressed his sleeping acupoint.

Geom Mugeuk loaded the sleeping Jaein into the back of the carriage. It was a small carriage with almost no space for luggage, but there was enough room to lay Jaein down.

“Will you come with us?”

“Who is there?”

Geom Mugeuk swept his hand down from his forehead to his chin, then drew a smiling face.

Goeak could tell that the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon was there.

Goeak waved his hands and even shook his head, saying he wanted nothing to do with Demon Venerables.

“See you next time.”

He was about to leave when Geom Mugeuk said to him,

“Thank you.”

“For what?”

“I know that you accepted their request on purpose because of me.”

Without looking back, Goeak answered,

“I told you it was because of the money. It makes my stomach hurt to think about it, so stop talking about it.”

Goeak then launched himself away and vanished.

Looking after him, Geom Mugeuk smiled faintly.

‘Why do I talk so much, you ask? Was it not so that I could meet you in a place like this today?’

Because they had come to know each other’s true hearts, Goeak had stepped forward to help him today. If he had treated him taciturnly, how could they have known each other’s feelings?

In these days, when severing ties was becoming easier and easier, that was why Geom Mugeuk still talked a lot.

“Now then, let us go as well.”

“Yes!”

Chohui silently watched Geom Mugeuk drive the carriage.

Goeak, and Gwisaldae as well. Those terrifying names were turning into ordinary names when they came to this Young Cult Leader.

And one more conclusion she reached.

The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult was not a talkative man. This sight of him silently looking only ahead as he drove the carriage suited him far more than the talkative 모습 he had shown earlier.

* * *

At last, the two of them arrived at Cheonhwaru.

Ahead, they saw the figure of Cheonhwaru’s Tower Lord, Yeojeong.

At the sight of the Tower Lord, whom she had been worried about, safe and well, tears welled up in Chohui’s eyes.

“Tower Lord!”

Seeing Yeojeong, Chohui tried to offer a deep bow on the spot. But Yeojeong came to her first and took her hand.

Yeojeong led her over and seated her in her own place. Then she brought water and had her drink.

“Drink some water first. Slowly.”

“Yes, Tower Lord.”

After calming the excited girl, Yeojeong first examined her body.

“Are you hurt anywhere?”

“I am fine.”

Despite her answer, Yeojeong calmly examined her body. It was obvious she would say she was not hurt even if she was, so Yeojeong was checking carefully herself.

One might have expected her to ask first what information she had brought, but she looked after Chohui’s body first.

Naturally, the look in Chohui’s eyes as she gazed at the Tower Lord deepened.

“You did this that day too.”

“That day?”

“The day you saved my life.”

Yeojeong had saved her when she was being sold beyond the borders because of her father’s gambling debts. It had truly been a chance meeting on the road.

Back then, Yeojeong had seated her, as she cried and resisted, shaking off the men’s arms and refusing to go, and had her drink water just like today. After calming her excitement, she asked what had happened.

Yeojeong paid off Chohui’s debt on the spot, then simply went on her way.

If she had made Chohui work at Cheonhwaru after saving her, Chohui would not have respected Yeojeong like this.

“As you left, you said this to me. Since my parents had abandoned me, I should no longer be bound by family. You told me to leave home and live with a dream of my own.”

That day, Chohui followed Yeojeong. Even when she was told that following her would make her a courtesan, she had entrusted her fate to Yeojeong.

“Did I say that?”

Though she said that, Yeojeong remembered that day vividly.

“It was not I who saved you. Fate saved you.”

That fate had continued on to this very place today.

“You went through much to come here.”

The moment she heard those words, the tears Chohui had been holding back streamed down her face. It truly would not be an exaggeration to call it a miracle that she had come all the way here alive.

“Are you all right, Tower Lord?”

“I am fine. Are there not people over there protecting us?”

Only then did Chohui look around. Faceless Guests stood on all sides, guarding the courtesan house.

When the two of them arrived, those who had been in the rooms came out and began guarding the place. Their mission had changed.

“Could they be……?”

Chohui knew as well. She knew who wore those masks.

Naturally, her gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk and the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon, who were conversing a little ways away.

‘Then that man must be the terrifying Extreme Evil Smiling Demon.’

Chohui was seeing the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon for the first time. Was it because she thought of him as the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon? The smiling white mask felt especially frightening.

The sight of Geom Mugeuk smiling brightly in front of that Extreme Evil Smiling Demon felt unreal. Once again, she felt that every danger became ordinary once it passed through the Young Cult Leader.

“If not for the Young Cult Leader over there, I would have died.”

Yeojeong smiled faintly.

‘The same was true on this side.’

Chohui continued speaking.

“The Young Cult Leader said so. He said he was close enough to you, Tower Lord, that even if the entire murim blocked him, he would break through and come find you.”

At that, Yeojeong’s gaze turned toward the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon.

“It is not because of me, but because of that person.”

From the look in Yeojeong’s eyes as she gazed at him, Chohui could sense that the relationship between the two of them was deep. It was a woman’s intuition.

‘Ah!’

Only now did she understand. What it meant that he had come not for Yeojeong’s sake, but because of the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon. Since Yeojeong had a deep relationship with the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon, the Young Cult Leader had come running for the Tower Lord!

After looking at the two of them, Chohui turned back to the Tower Lord of Cheonhwaru. Now it was time to convey the reason she had risked her life to come here.

“There is something I must tell you, Tower Lord.”

She spoke in a low voice so that only the Tower Lord of Cheonhwaru could hear.

“A great incident that will turn the murim upside down is going to happen at Cheonhwaru!”

She lowered her voice even further and said,

“And the person who will cause that incident is…… you, Tower Lord.”

Chapter 535: You Messed With Us, So Be Prepared for That Much
-------------------------------------------------------------
“That was why I couldn’t tell anyone.”

Because this affair was deeply connected to Cheonhwaru—and to the Mistress of Cheonhwaru. She had feared that if she spoke carelessly, an even greater calamity might befall the Tower Mistress.

She had expected Yeojeong to be greatly shocked, but the Mistress of Cheonhwaru was, surprisingly, not badly shaken.

Yes. A woman who lived with the fate of loving Geugak Soma would surely meet storms of this degree head-on.

“From now on, I’ll tell those two and discuss it with them. Will that be all right?”

In truth, it was not permission she strictly needed to ask for. Even so, she sought Chohui’s consent, for Chohui had risked her life to bring this information to her.

Chohui knew well that the Tower Mistress treated her subordinates with such consideration. Yeojeong herself probably did not know how much comfort and strength a single word like this—one that could have been passed over—gave to those beneath her.

Yeojeong and Chohui approached Geom Mugeuk and Geugak Soma. The two were in the midst of conversation.

“It would have been nice if Soma-nim had tasted Father’s fish dish too.”

When Geugak Soma imagined the Cult Leader losing bet after bet and cooking fish, laughter came to him of its own accord.

“It was more enjoyable than any journey I’ve taken until now.”

Geugak Soma responded to Geom Mugeuk’s words with a joke.

“I’m hurt. I thought the journey you took with me was the best.”

“Records exist to be broken. Come travel with me again.”

Just then, Yeojeong cut in and said,
